## Page 9

UW SOURCE: Eligibility Processing – Part 5 
About this Manual 9 
ABOUT THIS MANUAL 
This manual is intended to be a guide and reference manual for using the [[Underwriting]] (UW) SOURCE 
application.  
This manual familiarizes end-users with the UW SOURCE application, and explains the tasks that can be 
performed in [[Underwriting]] offerings, issues, and [[securities]]. It also provides descriptions for the fields in the UW 
SOURCE interface. Each part focuses on a specific area. 
• Offering Activity Processing - Part 1 
• Offering Activity Processing - Part 2 
• Dashboards, Work Plans and Work Items, and User Hierarchy - Part 3 
• Queries and Reports - Part 4 
• Eligibility Processing - Part 5 
Intended Audience 
This manual is for the users of UW SOURCE application, such as The [[Depository Trust Company]] ([[DTC]]) staff, 
underwriters, correspondents, transfer agents, [[issuer]], and counsel of [[issuer]], who need to perform various 
tasks to process deals at [[DTC]] using UW SOURCE.  
UW SOURCE Phases 
Municipal Sub Issue Type Release 1 
(Consists of Municipal Bond, Municipal Note, Municipal VRDO, Municipal Other Tax Exempt 
(MOTE), Municipal Insured Custodial Receipt (MICR)) 
Went live in 
September 2008. 
Corporate Sub Issue Type Release 2 
(Consists of Common [[Stock]], Preferred [[Stock]], [[Equity]] Derivative, IMMI, Closed End Fund, [[ADR]], 
Limited Partnership) 
Went live in 
October 2009. 
Corporate Sub Issue Type Release 3 
(Consists of Corporate Bond, Certificates of Deposit (CD), Collateralized Mortgage Obligation 
(CMO), [[Asset]]-Backed Security ([[ABS]]), Corporate VRDO, Government Security, Corporate Debt 
Derivative) 
Went live in 
October 2010. 
Corporate Sub Issue Type Release 4 
(Consists of Corporate Insured custodial Receipt, Debt Unit, Depository [[shares]], [[Equity]] Unit, 
Exchange Traded Fund, Rights, Unit Investment Trust, [[Warrants]]) and Municipal Insured 
Custodial Receipt (Revised Version) 
Went live In 
November 2011.

Organization of Part 5 
Information in this manual has been organized as follows: 
Chapter Description 
Chapter 1 – Getting Started Provides an overview of UW SOURCE and with the process for logging on to

SOURCE.  
Chapter 3 - Agent Operational 
Arrangements Processing 
Describes all the tasks for [[transfer agent]] and Paying Agent operational 
arrangements processing with [[DTC]] in UW SOURCE.

Processing 
Describes all the tasks associated with Agent Confirmation Processing for 
Transfer Agents and Paying Agents. 
Chapter 5 - BLOR/ ILOR/ Riders 
Processing 
Describes all the tasks in Letter of Representations (LORs) processing such 
as creating, matching, and unmatching BLORs, ILORs, and Riders. 
Chapter 6 - Exchange Letter 
Processing 
Describes all the tasks related to creating, searching, and matching 
exchange letters in UW SOURCE. 
Chapter 7 - Eligibility QA 
Processes 
Describes all the QA related tasks such as QA review, issue approval and 
approving a security for [[settlement]] eligibility. 
Chapter 8 - Foreign Legal 
Opinion Compliance 
Describes all the tasks in reviewing an issue for foreign legal opinion 
compliance. 
Chapter 9 - Financial Action 
Task Force (FATF) Compliance 
Describes all the tasks in viewing and reviewing FATF compliance. 
Chapter 10 - Office Of Foreign 
Assets Control (OFAC) 
Compliance 
Describes all the tasks related to OFAC processing. 
Chapter 11 - Legal Review of 
Ownership Restricted Issues 
Describes all the tasks related to legal review of ownership restrictions such 
as approving, rejecting and returning ownership restrictions. 
Glossary Provides the Glossary.

## Page 12

UW SOURCE: Eligibility Processing – Part 5 
Getting Started 12 
1. GETTING STARTED 
The [[Depository Trust Company]] ([[DTC]]) provides [[custody]] and [[Asset]] servicing for more than two million [[securities]] 
from the US and 100 other countries for its participants and their customers. The [[Underwriting]] Service is the 
starting point for all [[securities]] offerings at [[DTC]]. Through the service's New Issue Eligibility program, underwriters 
can quickly and economically distribute primary and [[Secondary Offerings]] of [[securities]] via electronic book- entry 
delivery and [[settlement]]. These newly issued [[securities]] are then available for the full range of depository services. 
UW SOURCE is a new platform, replacing the legacy [[Underwriting]] systems at [[DTC]]. The integrated platform 
facilitates the streamlining of the [[Underwriting]] process to generate further efficiencies, reduce costs and provide 
greater flexibility. UW SOURCE improves the [[Underwriting]] processing in the following functional areas: 
• [[Underwriting]] Offering Activity Processing 
• Eligibility Processing (Including the Quality Assurance Processing) 
• Security Eligibility Determination 
Logging in to UW SOURCE 
To log in to UW SOURCE: 
1. In the address bar of the browser, type in the URL for the MyDTCC portal   
• Internal User: https://portal.[[DTCC]].org 
• External User: https://portal.[[DTCC]].com 
2. From the Login page that appears: 
i. Enter your Login [[ID]] and Password. 
ii. Click Login.

[[Underwriting]] System tile. 
 
The UW SOURCE Welcome page appears.

## Page 14

UW SOURCE: Eligibility Processing – Part 5 
Common Tasks 14 
2. COMMON TASKS 
This chapter explains the common actions and tasks that can be performed in UW SOURCE . You can find 
assistance on using the Help feature of UW SOURCE. 
You can perform actions such as Reset, Cancel, Save, and Submit that are common to most of the pages in UW 
SOURCE. The list of common actions and the purpose of each is described in the following table. 
Table 1: Common Actions in UW SOURCE 
Action Name Purpose 
Reset Clears the previously entered details in the page. 
Cancel Navigates user back to previous page. 
Save Saves the details entered in the page in UW SOURCE 
Submit Saves the information entered in UW SOURCE after necessary validations. 
Search Displays the results of the entered search criteria 
Go Opens a new page with the list of Search results 
Print Launches a printer friendly page for Search results or other tabular data and enables you to 
print a page. 
 
You can perform common tasks such as Modify, Delete, View, Add, Open Query and Save Query that behave 
similarly across the user interface in UW SOURCE. 
The list of common tasks and the purpose of each is described in the following table: 
Table 2: Common Functions in UW SOURCE 
Action Name Purpose and Description 
Modify Modifies the details of the selected record in the list.  
Delete Deletes the selected record from the list. 
View Displays the details of a record in the list.  
Add Adds the details entered for the record to the list. 
Open Query Accesses the personalized queries  
Save Query Searches and saves the query in UW SOURCE.

## Page 15

UW SOURCE: Eligibility Processing – Part 5 
Common Tasks 15 
Using Help 
The help in UW SOURCE is context-sensitive. Press the F1 key to access help. The Contents, Index, and Search 
tabs display helpful information that you can print or copy.  
To access Help in UW SOURCE: 
1. From the menu bar, select Help, or press the F1 key. 
2. Click the Search tab to display the Search panel. 
3. In the Search panel, enter the search criteria, and click Search. 
 A topic list appears. 
4. Click a search result link to display the corresponding topic.  
 
Figure 1: Accessing Help 
Agent Operational Arrangements Processing 
This process ensures that the [[transfer agent]] and paying agent have valid Operational Arrangements ([[OA]]) with 
[[DTC]]. 


---

## Page 16

UW SOURCE: Eligibility Processing – Part 5 
Creating Agent Operational Arrangements 16 
3. CREATING AGENT OPERATIONAL ARRANGEMENTS 
 
[[DTC]] staff captures electronic copies of the [[OA]] through UW SOURCE. UW SOURCE requests the status of the 
agent’s [[OA]] details from the Entity Master File (EMF).  
If [[OA]] is not available for the agent, create an [[OA]] record. The Eligibility Specialist reviews the signed [[OA]] 
document received from the agent. This review is done to ensure that the document is in the correct format and 
contains the required details. The Eligibility Specialist then approves the document and creates an [[OA]] record in 
UW SOURCE. This is a legal document, which is then scanned and attached to the record in UW SOURCE. 
To create agent operational arrangements: 
1. From the Eligibility menu, choose Operational Arrangements -> Create [[OA]]. 
 The Create Agent Operational Arrangements page appears. 
 
Figure 2: Create Agent Operational Arrangements Page 
  


---

## Page 17

UW SOURCE: Eligibility Processing – Part 5 
Creating Agent Operational Arrangements 17 
2. In the Authorized Agent Information section, enter the following mandatory fields: 
• Agent [[ID]] 
• Agent Name.  
• Authorized Officer First Name 
• Authorized Officer Last Name 
• Phone 
• Extn 
• Email [[ID]]  
3. Optional step - Enter Address Line 1, Address Line 2, City, Zip Code and the State and Country. 
4. In the [[OA]] Document Information section, enter the Document Date. 
5. Optional step - Attach the [[OA]] document.  
 To attach the [[OA]] document: 
• Click Attach. 
 A pop-up window appears. 
• Click Browse and select the required document.  
• Click Submit. 
6. Optional step - Enter the Received Date and Received User.  
7. Optional - In the Associated Agent List, add the Agents. 
8. Optional step - In the Approval Information section, add internal comments. 
9. Click Submit.  
Note: Alternatively, click Save if you would want to edit the created agent [[OA]] information. If you click 
Submit, you cannot edit the [[OA]] information. 
Create Agent Operational Arrangements Page 
The Create Agent Operational Arrangements page enables you to create agent operational arrangement 
details. 
The sections available on this page are: 
• Identification Information 
• Authorized Agent Information 
• [[OA]] Document Information 
• Response Information

## Page 18

UW SOURCE: Eligibility Processing – Part 5 
Creating Agent Operational Arrangements 18 
Identification Information Section 
This section displays information regarding the operational arrangement [[ID]] and its status. 
Table 3: Identification Information Section 
Field Name Field Description 
[[OA]] [[ID]] The unique [[ID]] assigned by the UW SOURCE for identifying an [[OA]]. 
[[OA]] Status The status of [[OA]]. 
Valid values: 
• Active 
Indicates that an active Operational Arrangements exists for the agent. 
• Inactive 
Indicates that the Operational Arrangements was deactivated for the 
agent and the information is available only for query purposes. 
• Draft 
Indicates that the Operational Arrangements is saved but not submitted 
 
Authorized Agent Information Section 
This section enables you to create the details of the authorized agent for the operational arrangement.  
Table 4: Authorized Agent Information Section 
Field Name Field Description 
Agent The identification of the agent who performs the transfer functions of registered 
[[securities]]. – OR – 
The identification of the agent who performs the payment functions of registered 
[[securities]]. 
Authorized Officer First 
Name 
The First name of the authorized officer.  
Authorized Officer Last 
Name 
The Last name of the authorized officer. 
Phone The phone number of the authorized officer. 
Extn The extension number of the authorized officer. 
Email [[ID]] The e-mail [[ID]] of the authorized officer. 
Address Line 1 The address details of the place of authorized officer. 
Address Line 2 The address details of the place of authorized officer. 
City The name of the city. 
State The name of the state. 
Non-US State The name of the non-US state if the officer is non-US. 
Zip Code The zip code of the state. 
Country The name of the country.

UW SOURCE: Eligibility Processing – Part 5 
Creating Agent Operational Arrangements 19 
[[OA]] Document Information Section 
This section enables you to create the information related to the operational arrangements and attach the 
necessary document if required. 
Table 5: [[OA]] Document Information Section 
Field Name Field Description 
Document Date  The date specified in the [[OA]] document, based on which a document is 
considered Official or Released. 
Received Date  The date on which the [[OA]] is received by [[DTC]] from the agent. 
Received User The name of the user who receives the [[OA]] from the agent. 
Document name Enables the user to attach the required document 
 
Response Information Section 
This section displays your User [[ID]]. You need to enter the internal comments stating the reason for creation of 
operational arrangements. 
Table 6: Response Information Section 
Field Name Field Description 
User [[ID]] The [[DTC]] Staff login details.  
Date The current date. 
Internal Comments Comments entered by the [[DTC]] staff for [[DTC]]’s own reference. 
Searching for Agent Operational Arrangements 
To search for agent operational arrangements: 
1. From the Eligibility menu, choose Operational Arrangements->Search [[OA]]. 
 The Search Agent Operational Arrangements page appears. 
 
Figure 3: Search Agent Operational Arrangements page 
2. In the Search Criteria section, enter the required search criteria, and click Search. 
 The Agent Operational Arrangements List section appears.

Viewing Operational Arrangements 
To view agent operational arrangements: 
1. From the Eligibility menu, choose Operational Arrangements->Search [[OA]]. 
 The Search Agent Operational Arrangements page appears. 
2. Enter the search criteria and click Search. 
 The Agent Operational Arrangements List section appears. 
3. Select the desired [[OA]]. 
4. From the Action drop-down list, select View [[OA]], and click Go. 
 The View Agent Operational Arrangements page appears. 
 
Figure 4: View Agent Operational Arrangements page

Maintaining Agent Operational Arrangements 
This chapter describes all the tasks to maintain agent operational arrangements. There may be times when the 
agent must modify some of the contact details on the valid Operational Arrangements ([[OA]]) record in UW 
SOURCE. Within UW SOURCE, the following three options are available for maintaining [[OA]] records: 
• Modify 
• Deactivate 
• Reactivate

Modifying Operational Arrangements

Deactivating [[OA]] 
As part of [[OA]] record maintenance, it may be necessary to deactivate an [[OA]] record. This action can only be 
processed by [[DTC]]. The need to deactivate an [[OA]] record arises when the agent is no longer willing to comply 
with the [[OA]]. 
To deactivate [[OA]]: 
1. From the Eligibility menu, choose Operational Arrangements->Search [[OA]]. 
 The Search Agent Operational Arrangements page appears. 
2. In the Search Criteria section, from the [[OA]] Status drop-down list, select Active and click Search. 
 The  Agent Operational Arrangements List section appears. 
3. Select an [[OA]]. 
4. From the Action drop-down list, select Deactivate [[OA]] and click Go. 
 The Deactivate Agent Operational Arrangements page appears.

Reactivating [[OA]]

request from the agent.

 The Search Agent Operational Arrangements page appears.

 The Agent Operational Arrangements List section appears. 
3. Select an [[OA]], and from the Action drop-down list, select Reactivate [[OA]], and click Go. 
 The Reactivate Agent Operational Arrangements page appears.

## Page 33

UW SOURCE: Eligibility Processing – Part 5 
Creating Agent Operational Arrangements 33 
Field Name Field Description 
A security that represents ownership in a corporation. Owners of 
Common [[Stock]] are on the bottom of the priority ladder of the ownership 
structure. 
• Preferred  [[Stock]] 
A class of ownership in a corporation with a stated [[Dividend]] that must be 
paid before dividends to owners of Common [[Stock]]. Preferred S tock 
owners usually [[DO]] not have [[voting rights]]. Preferred [[Stock]] holders carry 
additional rights above and beyond those conferred on a Common [[Stock]]. 
• Limited Partnership 
A limited partnership consists of one or more general partners, who 
manage the business and assume legal debts and [[obligations]], and one 
or more limited partners who are liable only to the extent of their 
investments. These investors are not liable for legal action or debts the 
company may face. 
• American Depositary Receipt ([[ADR]]) 
A negotiable security issued by a US commercial bank that represents 
a specified number of [[shares]] in a foreign [[Stock]] that is traded in a US 
exchange. ADRs are denominated in US dollars. 
• [[Equity]] [[Derivatives]] 
 A hybrid security whose value is based on the performance of the 
underlying instrument. [[Equity]] Derivative trades in [[shares]]/ units. 
• Closed End Fund 
A type of fund having a limited number of [[shares]] listed on a [[Stock]] 
exchange. Closed funds redeem [[shares]] on a continuous basis. 
• Institutional Money Market Instrument (IMMI) 
A mutual fund whose investments are in high-yield money market 
instruments such as federal [[securities]], CDs and commercial paper. Its 
intent is to make such instruments, normally purchased in large 
denominations by [[Institutions]],available indirectly to individuals.  
• [[Equity]] Unit 
A security usually comprised of two other [[securities]] (the components) 
traded together,  generally they consist of common [[Stock]] and a warrant. 
The unit can separate into the components immediately upon issuance 
or trade on its own until such time that the [[issuer]] decides to separate the 
unit into the components. The separation can be voluntary or mandatory.   
• Unit Investment Trust 

---

## Page 34

UW SOURCE: Eligibility Processing – Part 5 
Creating Agent Operational Arrangements 34 
Field Name Field Description 
An investment company that offers a fixed, unmanaged portfolio, 
generally of stocks and [[Bonds]], as redeemable "units" to investors for a 
specific period of time. It is designed to provide capital appreciation 
and/or [[Dividend]] income. 
• Exchange Traded Funds 
A security that tracks an index, a commodity or a basket of assets like 
an index fund, but trades like a [[Stock]] on an exchange. ETFs experience 
price changes throughout the day as they are bought and sold. 
• [[Warrants]] 
A warrant is a security that entitles its owner to buy a share of the 
issuing company's [[Stock]] at a predetermined price regardless of the 
[[Stock]]’s current market value.  [[Warrants]] are issued to enhance the 
future value of the [[Stock]] to the holder. 
• Depository [[shares]] 
A depository share represents ownership in the [[shares]] of a non-U.S. 
company that trades in U.S. financial markets. 
Like [[ADR]]'s Depository [[shares]] are created when [[shares]] of U.S. 
corporation are placed into a trust account or with a custodian bank.  
The [[securities]] issued are termed to be Depository [[shares]] 
• Rights 
A security giving stockholders [[Entitlement]] to purchase new [[shares]] 
issued by [[The Corporation]] at a predetermined price (normally less than 
the current market price) in proportion to the number of [[shares]] already 
owned. Rights are issued only for a short period of time, after which 
they expire. 
• Corporate Bond 
A debt obligation or IOU issued by public or private corporation that 
obligates the [[issuer]] to pay the bondholder principal plus interest. 
• Corporate VRDO  
Corporate Variable Rate Demand [[obligations]] (VRDO) is floating rate 
instrument. They pay interest monthly or quarterly based on a floating 
rate that is reset daily or weekly based on an index of short-term 
corporate debt rates. 
• Certificates of Deposit (CD) 

---

## Page 35

UW SOURCE: Eligibility Processing – Part 5 
Creating Agent Operational Arrangements 35 
Field Name Field Description 
CDs are time deposit products that are FDIC insured and have flexible 
maturity periods ranging from seven days to 14 years. 
• Debt Unit 
A security usually comprised of two other [[securities]] (the components) 
traded together,  generally they consist of corporate bond and a warrant. 
The unit can separate into the components immediately upon issuance 
or trade on its own until such time that the [[issuer]] decides to separate the 
unit into the components. The separation can be voluntary or mandatory.  
• Corporate Insured Custodial Receipt 
Corporate [[Bonds]] are insured by insurance [[Companies]]. Corporate insured 
receipts represent the insured portion and ar e similar to the original 
corporate bond except for the security identification number. 
• Collateralized Mortgage Obligation(CMO) 
A type of mortgage backed security. 
• [[Asset]]-Backed Security ([[ABS]])  
A type of security backed by a pool of credit card receivables, home-
[[Equity]] loans, or other assets. 
• Government Security 
[[securities]] issued by any government agency.  
• Corporate Debt Derivative  
 A hybrid security whose value is based on the performance of the 
underlying instrument. Debt Derivative trades in the denominated 
currency. 
• Municipal Bond 
A debt obligation issued by municipality or local government agency. 
• Municipal VRDO 
Municipal Variable Rate Demand [[obligations]] (VRDO) is floating rate 
instruments with terms of as much as 40 years. They pay interest 
monthly or quarterly based on a floating rate that is reset daily or weekly 
based on an index of short-term municipal rates. 
• Municipal Note 
Shorter maturity period between issuance and redemption than a 
municipal bond. Maturity period is one year or less.  

---

## Page 36

UW SOURCE: Eligibility Processing – Part 5 
Creating Agent Operational Arrangements 36 
Field Name Field Description 
• Municipal Insured Custodial Receipt 
Municipal [[Bonds]] are insured by American Municipal Bond Assurance 
Corporation (AMBAC) or MBIA. A municipal insured custodial receipt 
represents the insured portion and is similar to the original municipal 
bond except for the security identification number. 
• Municipal Other Tax Exempt 
Municipal Other Tax Exempt is debt [[obligations]] that are issued by the 
state, county, city, or any other political sub- division. These [[Bonds]]  are 
attractive to individual investors, since, as the name indicates, the 
government does not tax the interest received.

## Page 38

UW SOURCE: Eligibility Processing – Part 5 
Agent Confirmation Processing 38 
4. AGENT CONFIRMATION PROCESSING 
This chapter describes all the tasks for Agent Confirmation processing. As part of making a security ‘[[settlement]] 
eligible,’ [[DTC]] needs to confirm that the agent is in agreement with the issue details. 
During Agent [[ID]] confirmation, UW SOURCE sends the issue information to the agents so that they may confirm 
the agent [[ID]] listed. UW SOURCE  provides an online facility through which agents can review the issue 
information and confirm the Agent [[ID]] and issue details.  
UW SOURCE handles the agent confirmation process in two steps: 
1. Agent [[ID]] confirmation – Agents confirm that they are the agent for the issue and the Agent [[ID]] identified by 
the [[underwriter]] in the issue. 
2. Issue details confirmation – Agent confirms that the issue details submitted by the [[underwriter]] are correct. 
In addition to issue details confirmation, the [[transfer agent]] also enters the transfer fee. Agent Confirmation 
processing is done independently for transfer agents and paying agents. 
 UW SOURCE sends the issue information to agents for Agent [[ID]] confirmation when both the following 
conditions are satisfied: 
• The Agent [[ID]] is present, and is not a Dummy Agent [[ID]], nor Internal Agent [[ID]]. 
• The Agent Operational Arrangements processing for the agent is successful. 
  

---

Confirming the [[transfer agent]] or Paying Agent [[ID]]  
As part of making a security ‘[[settlement]] eligible,’ [[DTC]] needs to confirm that the agent is in agreement with the 
issue details. 
During Agent [[ID]] confirmation, UW SOURCE sends the issue information to the agents so that they may confirm 
the agent [[ID]] listed. 
The agents review the issue information and perform one of the following actions: 
• Confirm that they are the agent for the issue and that the given Agent [[ID]] is correct. 

---

## Page 46

UW SOURCE: Eligibility Processing – Part 5 
Agent Confirmation Processing 46 
• Confirm that they are the agent for the issue but change the listed agent [[ID]] to another [[ID]] from  within the 
same organization. This alteration is most often performed when the [[ID]] listed is valid for the agent, but is 
incorrect for the specific issue. Agents typically maintain numerous valid agent IDs. 
• Reject the issue when they are not the agent for the issue. 
If the required issue details are available during Agent [[ID]] confirmation, the agent can confirm the Agent [[ID]] and 
issue information at the same time. 
To confirm the [[transfer agent]] or paying agent [[ID]]: 
1. From the Eligibility menu, choose Agent Confirmation.  
2. In the Agent Confirmation section, select Pending For Agent [[ID]] Confirmation from the Agent 
Confirmation Status drop-down list. 
3. In the Issue List section, select the desired issue.  
4. From the Action drop-down list, select Confirm TA [[ID]] or Confirm PA [[ID]]. 
 The [[transfer agent]] [[ID]] Confirmation page or the Paying Agent [[ID]] Confirmation page appears. 
 
  
Figure 10: [[transfer agent]] [[ID]] Confirmation Page 


---

Agent Confirmation Processing 47 
5. In the Issue List section, select the desired Confirm TA [[ID]] or Confirm PA [[ID]]. 
6. Verify the issue information displayed in this page. 
7. In the Agent [[ID]] Confirmation section, select the desired [[Option]]. 
• Confirm With Same Agent [[ID]] if the given agent [[ID]] is correct. 
• Confirm With A Different Agent [[ID]] if the given agent is correct and agent [[ID]] is wrong. 
• Reject As I Am Not The Agent if the agent [[ID]] given is wrong. 
8. In the Security List section, select the desired security and click View to view the security details. 
9. Click Back To Agent [[ID]] Confirmation after viewing the security details. 
10. Enter External Comments and click Submit.  
 The message, [[transfer agent]] [[ID]] Confirmed Successfully, is displayed.

Table 17: Issue Identification Information section 
Field Name Field Description 
Issue [[ID]] The unique identifier assigned to the issue by UW SOURCE. 
Sub Issue Type The type of Security. 
Valid values: 
• Common [[Stock]] 
A security that represents ownership in a corporation. Owners of 
Common [[Stock]] are on the bottom of the priority ladder of the ownership 
structure. 
• Preferred  [[Stock]] 
A class of ownership in a corporation with a stated [[Dividend]] that must be 
paid before dividends to owners of Common [[Stock]]. Preferred [[Stock]]

## Page 48

UW SOURCE: Eligibility Processing – Part 5 
Agent Confirmation Processing 48 
Field Name Field Description 
owners usually [[DO]] not have [[voting rights]]. Preferred [[Stock]] holders carry 
additional rights above and beyond those conferred on a Common [[Stock]]. 
• Limited Partnership 
A limited partnership consists of one or more general partners, who 
manage the business and assume legal debts and [[obligations]], and one 
or more limited partners who are liable only to the extent of their 
investments. These investors are not liable for legal action or debts the 
company may face. 
• American Depositary Receipt ([[ADR]]) 
A negotiable security issued by a US commercial bank that represents 
a specified number of [[shares]] in a foreign [[Stock]] that is traded in a US 
exchange. ADRs are denominated in US dollars. 
• [[Equity]] [[Derivatives]] 
 A hybrid security whose value is based on the performance of the 
underlying instrument. [[Equity]] Derivative trades in [[shares]]/ units. 
• Closed End Fund 
A type of fund having a limited number of [[shares]] listed on a [[Stock]] 
exchange. Closed funds redeem [[shares]] on a continuous basis. 
• Institutional Money Market Instrument (IMMI) 
A mutual fund whose investments are in high-yield money market 
instrumentssuch as federal [[securities]], CDs and commercial paper. Its 
intent is tomake such instruments, normally purchased in large 
denominations by [[Institutions]],available indirectly to individuals.  
• [[Equity]] Unit 
A security usually comprised of two other [[securities]] (the components) 
traded together,  generally they consist of common [[Stock]] and a warrant. 
The unit can separate into the components immediately upon issuance 
or trade on its own until such time that the [[issuer]] decides to separate the 
unit into the components. The separation can be voluntary or mandatory. 
• Unit Investment Trust 
An investment company that offers a fixed, unmanaged portfolio, 
generally of stocks and [[Bonds]], as redeemable "units" to investors for a 
specific period of time. It is designed to provide capital appreciation 
and/or [[Dividend]] income.

## Page 49

UW SOURCE: Eligibility Processing – Part 5 
Agent Confirmation Processing 49 
Field Name Field Description 
• Exchange Traded Funds 
A security that tracks an index, a commodity or a basket of assets like 
an index fund, but trades like a [[Stock]] on an exchange. ETFs experience 
price changes throughout the day as they are bought and sold. 
• [[Warrants]] 
A warrant is a security that entitles its owner to buy a share of the 
issuing company's [[Stock]] at a predetermined price regardless of the 
[[Stock]]’s current market value.  [[Warrants]] are issued to enhance the 
future value of the [[Stock]] to the holder. 
• Depository [[shares]] 
A depository share represents ownership in the [[shares]] of a non-U.S. 
company that trades in U.S. financial markets. 
Like [[ADR]]'s Depository [[shares]] are created when [[shares]] of U.S. 
corporation are placed into a trust account or with a custodian bank.  
The [[securities]] issued are termed to be Depository [[shares]]. 
• Rights 
A security giving stockholders [[Entitlement]] to purchase new [[shares]] 
issued by [[The Corporation]] at a predetermined price (normally less than 
the current market price) in proportion to the number of [[shares]] already 
owned. Rights are issued only for a short period of time, after which 
they expire. 
• Corporate Bond 
A debt obligation or IOU issued by public or private corporation that 
obligates the [[issuer]] to pay the bondholder principal plus interest. 
• Corporate VRDO  
Corporate Variable Rate Demand [[obligations]] (VRDO) is floating rate 
instrument. They pay interest monthly or quarterly based on a floating 
rate that is reset daily or weekly based on an index of short-term 
corporate debt rates. 
• Certificates of Deposit (CD) 
CDs are time deposit products that are FDIC insured and have flexible 
maturity periods ranging from seven days to 14 years. 
• Debt Unit

[[transfer agent]] 
Confirmation Status 
The confirmation status of the [[transfer agent]]. 
Valid values: 
• Agent Not Present 
Indicates that an agent is not present. 
• Dummy Agent [[ID]] No Confirmation Performed 
Indicates that a Dummy Agent [[ID]] is present and an Agent Confirmation 
has not been performed. 
• Pending For [[OA]] Check Completion 
Indicates that an Operational Arrangement Check is not yet successful 
for the agent. [[OA]] check should be successful to start Agent confirmation 
process. 
• Pending For Agent [[ID]] Confirmation 
Indicates that an issue is pending with an agent for Agent [[ID]] 
Confirmation. 
• Pending for required information 
Indicates that the Agent [[ID]] Confirmation is successful but pending for 
required issue information that needs to be confirmed by the agent. 
• Rejected 
Indicates that an agent has rejected the issue stating that he is not t he 
Agent for the Issue. 
• Pending for Issue Details Confirmation 
Indicates that an issue is pending with an agent for issue details 
confirmation. 
• Confirmed 
Indicates that an agent has confirmed the Issue details. 
• Confirmed With Update 
Indicates that an agent has confirmed the issue details with certain 
updates to the information. 
• Not Applicable 
Indicates that the TA confirmation check process is not applicable for the 
issue.

Confirming Issue Details 
During agent confirmation processing, it is necessary for the agents to also provide key details about the issue, 
such as security processing and corporate action information.   
When the required issue details are available, UW SOURCE will send the issue details to the agent for 
confirmation.  
Agents can perform either of the following actions during issue information: 
• Confirm the issue details - When the agent reviews the issue and security information and finds that all the 
issue information is correct, the agent can confirm the issue details.  

---

## Page 55

UW SOURCE: Eligibility Processing – Part 5 
Agent Confirmation Processing 55 
• Confirm the issue details with an update - When the agent reviews the issue and security information and 
disagrees, the agent can update the information appropriately and confirm it. 
To confirm the issue details: 
1. From the Eligibility menu, choose Agent Confirmation.  
2. In the Agent Confirmation section, from the Agent Confirmation Status drop-down list, select Pending 
For Issue Details Information. 
3. In the Issue List section, select the desired issue. 
4. Scroll down to the bottom of the page and from the Action drop-down list, select Review Issue Details 
For [[transfer agent]] Confirmation, and click Go. 
 The Review Issue Details for [[transfer agent]] Confirmation page appears. 
 
 
Figure 11: Review Issue Details for [[transfer agent]] Confirmation 
5. In the Issue Level Confirmation Information section, in the Security  List section, select the desired 
security.  
6. Click Confirm. 
 The Confirm Issue Details page appears.

## Page 63

UW SOURCE: Eligibility Processing – Part 5 
Agent Confirmation Processing 63 
Pushing Issue details for [[transfer agent]] or Paying Agent 
Confirmation 
In certain cases, the Eligibility Specialist may choose to send the issue and security information for issue details 
information even before all the required information is available. In these cases, the Specialist uses this [[Option]] 
to send the information to the agent for confirmation. The ‘Push’ must be exercised for each agent role separately.  
1. In the Search Issue For Agent Confirmation page, select Push Issue Details For [[transfer agent]] 
Confirmation or Push Issue Details For Paying Agent confirmation and click Go. 
 The Push Issue Details for [[transfer agent]] Confirmation or Push Issue Details For Paying Agent 
confirmation page appears. 
 
Figure 13: Push Issue Details for [[transfer agent]] Confirmation Page 
 
2. In the Security List section, select the desired security and click View to view the security information. 
3. Enter Internal Comments and External Comments, and click Submit.

## Page 64

UW SOURCE: Eligibility Processing – Part 5 
Agent Confirmation Processing 64 
The Push Issue Details For Confirmation Page 
The Push Issue Details For Paying Agent Confirmation page enables you to send the issue and security 
information for issue details information even before the required information is available. 
The sections available on this page are: 
• Issue Identification Information 
Table 23: The Issue Identification Information Section 
Field Name Field Description 
Issue [[ID]] The unique identifier assigned to the issue by UW SOURCE. 
Sub Issue Type The type of Security. 
Valid values: 
• Common [[Stock]] 
A security that represents ownership in a corporation. Owners of 
Common [[Stock]] are on the bottom of the priority ladder of the ownership 
structure. 
• Preferred  [[Stock]] 
A class of ownership in a corporation with a stated [[Dividend]] that must be 
paid before dividends to owners of Common [[Stock]]. Preferred S tock 
owners usually [[DO]] not have [[voting rights]]. Preferred [[Stock]] holders carry 
additional rights above and beyond those conferred on a Common [[Stock]]. 
• Limited Partnership 
A limited partnership consists of one or more general partners, who 
manage the business and assume legal debts and [[obligations]], and one 
or more limited partners who are liable only to the extent of their 
investments. These investors are not liable for legal action or debts the 
company may face. 
• American Depositary Receipt ([[ADR]]) 
A negotiable security issued by a US commercial bank that represents 
a specified number of [[shares]] in a foreign [[Stock]] that is traded in a US 
exchange. ADRs are denominated in US dollars. 
• [[Equity]] [[Derivatives]] 
 A hybrid security whose value is based on the performance of the 
underlying instrument. [[Equity]] Derivative trades in [[shares]]/ units. 
• Closed End Fund 
A type of fund having a limited number of [[shares]] listed on a [[Stock]] 
exchange. Closed funds redeem [[shares]] on a continuous basis. 
• Institutional Money Market Instrument (IMMI) 
A mutual fund whose investments are in high-yield money market 

---

## Page 65

UW SOURCE: Eligibility Processing – Part 5 
Agent Confirmation Processing 65 
Field Name Field Description 
instrumentssuch as federal [[securities]], CDs and commercial paper. Its 
intent is tomake such instruments, normally purchased in large 
denominations by [[Institutions]],available indirectly to individuals.  
• [[Equity]] Unit 
A security usually comprised of two other [[securities]] (the components) 
traded together,  generally they consist of common [[Stock]] and a warrant. 
The unit can separate into the components immediately upon issuance 
or trade on its own until such time that the [[issuer]] decides to separate the 
unit into the components. The separation can be voluntary or mandatory. 
• Unit Investment Trust 
An investment company that offers a fixed, unmanaged portfolio, 
generally of stocks and [[Bonds]], as redeemable "units" to investors for a 
specific period of time. It is designed to provide capital appreciation 
and/or [[Dividend]] income. 
• Exchange Traded Funds 
A security that tracks an index, a commodity or a basket of assets like 
an index fund, but trades like a [[Stock]] on an exchange. ETFs experience 
price changes throughout the day as they are bought and sold. 
• [[Warrants]] 
A warrant is a security that entitles its owner to buy a share of the 
issuing company's [[Stock]] at a predetermined price regardless of the 
[[Stock]]’s current market value.  [[Warrants]] are issued to enhance the 
future value of the [[Stock]] to the holder. 
• Depository [[shares]] 
A depository share represents ownership in the [[shares]] of a non-U.S. 
company that trades in U.S. financial markets. 
Like [[ADR]]'s Depository [[shares]] are created when [[shares]] of U.S. 
corporation are placed into a trust account or with a custodian bank.  
The [[securities]] issued are termed to be Depository [[shares]]. 
• Rights 
A security giving stockholders [[Entitlement]] to purchase new [[shares]] 
issued by [[The Corporation]] at a predetermined price (normally less than 
the current market price) in proportion to the number of [[shares]] already 
owned. Rights are issued only for a short period of time, after which 
they expire. 

---

## Page 66

UW SOURCE: Eligibility Processing – Part 5 
Agent Confirmation Processing 66 
Field Name Field Description 
• Corporate Bond 
A debt obligation or IOU issued by public or private corporation that 
obligates the [[issuer]] to pay the bondholder principal plus interest. 
• Corporate VRDO  
Corporate Variable Rate Demand [[obligations]] (VRDO) is floating rate 
instrument. They pay interest monthly or quarterly based on a floating 
rate that is reset daily or weekly based on an index of short-term 
corporate debt rates. 
• Certificates of Deposit (CD) 
CDs are time deposit products that are FDIC insured and have flexible 
maturity periods ranging from seven days to 14 years. 
• Debt Unit 
A security usually comprised of two other [[securities]] (the components) 
traded together; 
generally they consist of corporate bond and a warrant. 
The unit can separate into the components immediately upon issuance 
or trade on its own until such time that the [[issuer]] decides to separate 
the unit into the components. The separation can be voluntary or 
mandatory.  
• Corporate Insured Custodial Receipt 
Corporate [[Bonds]] are insured by insurance [[Companies]]. Corporate 
insured receipts represent the insured portion and are similar to the 
original corporate bond except for the security identification number. 
• Collateralized Mortgage Obligation(CMO) 
A type of mortgage backed security. 
• [[Asset]]-Backed Security ([[ABS]])  
A type of security backed by a pool of credit card receivables, home-
[[Equity]] loans, or other assets. 
• Government Security 
[[securities]] issued by any government agency.  
• Corporate Debt Derivative  
 A hybrid security whose value is based on the performance of the 
underlying instrument. Debt Derivative trades in the denominated 
currency. 

---

## Page 67

UW SOURCE: Eligibility Processing – Part 5 
Agent Confirmation Processing 67 
Field Name Field Description 
• Municipal Bond 
A debt obligation issued by municipality or local government agency. 
• Municipal VRDO 
Municipal Variable Rate Demand [[obligations]] (VRDO) is floating rate 
instruments with terms of as much as 40 years. They pay interest 
monthly or quarterly based on a floating rate that is reset daily or weekly 
based on an index of short-term municipal rates. 
• Municipal Note 
Shorter maturity period between issuance and redemption than 
amunicipal bond. Maturity period is one year or less.  
• Municipal Insured Custodial Receipt 
Municipal [[Bonds]] are insured by American Municipal Bond Assurance 
Corporation (AMBAC) or MBIA. A municipal insured custodial receipt 
represents the insured portion and is similar to the original municipal 
bond except for the security identification number. 
• Municipal Other Tax Exempt 
Municipal Other Tax Exempt is debt [[obligations]] that are issued by the 
state, county, city, or any other political sub- division. These [[Bonds]] are  
attractive to individual investors, since, as the name indicates, the 
government does not tax the interest received. 
[[transfer agent]] [[OA]] status The [[OA]] status of the [[transfer agent]]. 
Valid values: 
• Active 
Indicates that an active Operational Arrangements exists for the agent. 
• Inactive 
Indicates that the Operational Arrangements was deactivated for the 
agent and the information is available only for query purposes. 
• Draft 
Indicates that the Operational Arrangements is saved but not submitted. 
Series [[ID]] A identifier to uniquely identify a collection of [[securities]] within an Issue with same 
Expected Closing Date

## Page 70

UW SOURCE: Eligibility Processing – Part 5 
Agent Confirmation Processing 70 
Overriding the Agent Confirmation Process 
There are times during the agent confirmation process where [[DTC]] may be unable to get in touch with the agent 
and secure a confirmation. In such cases, the [[DTC]] QA Supervisor may choose to override the agent confirmation 
process and proceed with the QA review. 
Note: The agent is expected to confirm the information before the issue is closed. After issue closing, the agent 
can only ‘confirm’ the information; UW SOURCE does not allow the agent to perform a ‘con firm with update’ or 
‘reject’ the information. 
To override the agent confirmation process: 
1. In the Search Issue for Agent Confirmation page, select Override [[transfer agent]] Confirmation or 
Override Paying Agent Confirmation and click Go. 
The Override [[transfer agent]] Confirmation Process for Issue Approval and Closing or Override 
[[transfer agent]] Confirmation Process for Issue Approval and Closing page appears. 
 
Figure 14: Override Agent Confirmation Process for Issue Approval and Closing Page 
2. In the Security List section, select the desired security and click View to view the security information. 
3. Enter Internal Comments and External Comments, and click Submit. 


---

Resolving Agent Confirmation Mismatches 
The mismatch in issue information and the information entered by the agent can be resolved through this 
feature. 
To resolve agent confirmation mismatches: 
1. In the Search Issue For Agent Confirmation page, select Resolve Confirmation Mismatches and click 
Go. 
       The Resolve Agent Confirmation Mismatches page appears. 
 
 
Figure 15: Resolve Agent Confirmation Mismatches Page 
2. Select the security to be resolved and click Resolve. 
3. In the Issue Identification Information section select the appropriate value (Issue Value, TA Value or 
PA Value) to be taken. 
4. In the Security General Information section, in the Resolution column, from the drop-down list, select 
the appropriate resolution for the displayed fields and click Save. 


---

## Page 78

UW SOURCE: Eligibility Processing – Part 5 
Agent Confirmation Processing 78 
5. Optional step - If the Security Features section is applicable, click Continue To Interest Details, and 
select the appropriate resolution and click Save. 
6. Resolve other security feature information, if applicable. 
7. In the Search Issue for Agent Confirmation page, select Resolve Confirmation Mismatches and click 
Go. 
8. Navigate backwards to the Resolve Agent Confirmation Mismatches page and click Submit.

The unit can separate into the components immediately upon issuance 
or trade on its own until such time that the [[issuer]] decides to separate 
the unit into the components. The separation can be voluntary or 
mandatory.  
• Corporate Insured Custodial Receipt 
Corporate [[Bonds]] are insured by insurance [[Companies]]. Corporate 
insured receipts represent the insured portion and are similar to the 
original corporate bond except for the security identification number. 
• Collateralized Mortgage Obligation(CMO) 
A type of mortgage backed security. 
• [[Asset]]-Backed Security ([[ABS]])  

---

## Page 81

UW SOURCE: Eligibility Processing – Part 5 
Agent Confirmation Processing 81 
Field Name Field Description 
A type of security backed by a pool of credit card receivables, home-
[[Equity]] loans, or other assets. 
• Government Security 
[[securities]] issued by any government agency.  
• Corporate Debt Derivative  
 A hybrid security whose value is based on the performance of the 
underlying instrument. Debt Derivative trades in the denominated 
currency. 
• Municipal Bond 
A debt obligation issued by municipality or local government agency. 
• Municipal VRDO 
Municipal Variable Rate Demand [[obligations]] (VRDO) is floating rate 
instruments with terms of as much as 40 years. They pay interest 
monthly or quar
terly based on a floating rate that is reset daily or weekly 
based on an index of short-term municipal rates. 
• Municipal Note 
Shorter maturity period between issuance and redemption than 
amunicipal bond. Maturity period is one year or less.  
• Municipal Insured Custodial Receipt 
Municipal [[Bonds]] are insured by American Municipal Bond Assurance 
Corporation (AMBAC) or MBIA. A municipal insured custodial receipt 
represents the insured portion and is similar to the original municipal 
bond except for the security identification number. 
• Municipal Other Tax Exempt 
Municipal Other Tax Exempt is debt [[obligations]] that are issued by the 
state, county, city, or any other political sub- division. These [[Bonds]] are 
attractive to individual investors, since, as the name indicates , the 
government does not tax the interest received.

## Page 84

UW SOURCE: Eligibility Processing – Part 5 
Legal and Regulatory Processing 84 
5. LEGAL AND REGULATORY PROCESSING 
This chapter provides the steps for all the tasks for processing Blanket Letter of Representations (BLORs), [[issuer]] 
Letter of Representations (ILORs)/ Riders, and Exchange Letters. This process is common for corporate and 
municipal issues.  
Letter of Representations (LORs) are form agreements accepted by [[DTC]] from municipal and corporate issuers 
for book entry only ([[BEO]]) issues. These letters indicate that the [[issuer]] agrees to comply with the requirements 
stated in [[DTC]]’s Operational Arrangements ([[OA]]), as they may be amended from time to time.  
They contain a representation that must be made to [[DTC]] by the [[issuer]] and others (for example, trustee) before 
various issue types (for example, [[book-entry]]-only issues, [[Rule 144A]] issues, Reg. S issues) can be made eligible 
for deposit at [[DTC]]. 
Types of LORs 
There are two types of LORs:  
• Blanket Letter of Representations (BLOR) 
• [[issuer]] Letter of Representations (ILOR). 
Table 35: Types of Letter of Representation 
[[LOR]] Type Description 
Blanket Letter of 
Representations 
(BLOR) 
 
A BLOR is applicable to more than one issue. [[DTC]]  requires that an [[issuer]] 
submit a BLOR only once for all its future municipal or corporate issues that 
will be distributed through [[DTC]]. 
In the case of some tender [[Option]] municipal issues, a separate BLOR is 
required for every state. 
[[issuer]] Letter of 
Representations 
(ILOR) 
 
[[DTC]] requires an [[issuer]] to submit an ILOR each time they submit a [[BEO]] or 
144A issue for eligibility if a BLOR has not been submitted. 
An [[issuer]] submits a BLOR only once for all its future municipal or corporate 
issues distributed through [[DTC]]. An ILOR is submitted for each issue 
BLOR/ILOR and ILOR/Rider Processing 
BLOR/ ILOR processing in UW SOURCE is initiated under one of the following conditions: 
• BLOR or ILOR is received by [[DTC]] 
• Deal of type [[BEO]] is created in UW SOURCE, or deal is modified from type non-[[BEO]] to [[BEO]] 
• ILOR and Rider is received 
• Only Rider is received 
Creating BLOR

## Page 85

Legal and Regulatory Processing 85 
While BLOR, ILOR, and Rider this information is provided to [[DTC]] by issuers, it is typically communicated by fax 
or e-mail, not directly through UW SOURCE.  For this reason, it is necessary for [[DTC]] legal users to capture this 
information in the system. 
During the capture process, UW SOURCE saves all applicable ILOR and rider information in the offering record. 
• If the issue does not require riders and cannot be matched to a BLOR, the ILOR alone is captured in the 
offering record. 
• If the issue is successfully matched with a BLOR, but also requires riders, the riders alone are captured in 
the offering record.

1. From the Eligibility menu, choose BLOR Processing->Create BLOR. 
      The Create Blanket [[issuer]] Letter of Representations page appears.

Approving BLORs 
Once the [[LOR]] information has been captured in UW SOURCE, it must be approved by the legal user. Only 
approved BLORs and ILOR/Riders are considered to be eligible for matching with the issue.  
BLORs, ILORS and riders can exist in one of three versions: Draft, Signed Fax Copy, and Final.  This version 
has a direct impact on issue approval and closing at [[DTC]]. 
• A ‘Draft’ BLOR or ILOR is not eligible for issue approval or issue closing, 
• A ‘Signed fax copy’ BLOR or ILOR is eligible for issue approval but not for issue closing, and 
• A ‘Final’ BLOR or ILOR is eligible for both issue approval and issue closing. 
To approve a BLOR: 
1. From the Eligibility menu, choose BLOR Processing->Search BLOR. 
       The Search Blanket [[issuer]] Letter of Representations page appears. 
2. Enter the [[issuer]] [[ID]], and from the BLOR Status drop- down list, select Pending For Approval , and click 
Search. 
3. Select the BLOR to be approved, and from the Action drop-down list, select Review BLOR and click Go.  
      The Review Blanket [[issuer]] Letter of Representations page appears.

5. Enter the internal comments and click Submit.

Matching a BLOR 
 
• Once the [[LOR]] is approved, it is matched to a deal. During this process, BLORs (if applicable) are matched 
to the [[issuer]], while ILORs/ riders are matched to the issue. 
• A BLOR applies to multiple issues submitted by an [[issuer]] for [[DTC]] eligibility. This effectively eliminates the 
need for the [[issuer]] to prepare a new [[LOR]] for each new issue. When a new issue is submitted, the matching 
process validates that there is a BLOR detailing the operational requirements and procedures that are 
relevant to both the [[issuer]] and the agent. BLOR matching is a manual process performed by the [[DTC]] legal 
user through UW SOURCE . A BLOR is submitted at the [[issuer]] level; therefore the same BLOR can be 
matched to multiple issues associated with one [[issuer]]. 
Matching BLOR to Issue 
To match a BLOR to an issue: 
1. From the Eligibility menu, choose BLOR Processing  Search BLOR. 
The Search Blanket [[issuer]] Letter of Representations page appears. 
2. In the Search Criteria section, select the BLOR Status as Active, and click Search. 
3. In the Blanket [[issuer]] Letter of Representations List section, select the desired BLOR.  
4. From the Action drop-down list, select Match BLOR and click Go. 
The Select Issue for BLOR Matching page appears. 

---

UW SOURCE: Eligibility Processing – Part 5 
Legal and Regulatory Processing 107 
 
Figure 21: Select Issue for BLOR Matching Page 
 
5. Select an issue from the Potentially Matching Issue List and click Select Issue. 
The Match BLOR to Issue page appears. 
 
Figure 22: Match BLOR to Issue Page 
6. Click Submit.

Unmatching BLOR 
• If an issue is matched with an incorrect BLOR, UW SOURCE allows the [[DTC]] legal user to unmatch the issue 
and BLOR. 
• Once an issue has been matched to a BLOR, it cannot be matched to another BLOR. To correct this error, 
the [[DTC]] legal user must explicitly unmatch the issue and BLOR and then match the correct BLOR to the 
issue.  
• If the legal user needs to perform BLOR unmatching on an issue that has already been matched to an ILOR, 
the ILOR will be systemically unmatched as a part of this process.  In this circumstance there is no need to 
manually unmatch the ILOR. 
Unmatching Issue and BLOR 
To unmatch an issue and a BLOR: 
1. From the Eligibility menu, choose Search Issue. 
2. Enter the search criteria and click Search. 
3. In the Issue List section, select the desired issue. 
4. From the Action drop-down list, select Unmatch BLOR and click Go. 
The Unmatch Issue and BLOR page appears.

Approving ILOR/ Riders 
ILOR/ Riders can be approved in the following two ways. 
• During creation of ILOR / Riders the [[DTC]] legal user can approve the ILOR / Riders. 
• [[DTC]] legal user can review a BLOR and can either approve or reject the same. 
To approve ILOR/Riders during review: 
1. From the Eligibility menu, choose ILOR/Riders Processing->Search ILOR/Riders. 
The Search [[issuer]] Letter of Representations/Riders page appears. 
2. From the ILOR/Riders Status, select Pending For Approval and click Search. 
The [[issuer]] Letter of Representations List section appears. 
3. Select an ILOR, and from the Action drop-down list, select Review ILOR/Riders and click Go. 
The Review [[issuer]] Letter of Representations/Riders page appears.

Identification Number

Sub Issue Type Information Section 
Table 78: Sub Issue Type Information Section 
Field Name Field Description 
Sub Issue Type The type of Security. 
Valid values: 
• Common [[Stock]] 
A security that represents ownership in a corporation. Owners of Common 
[[Stock]] are on the bottom of the priority ladder of the ownership structure. 
• Preferred  [[Stock]] 
A class of ownership in a corporation with a stated [[Dividend]] that must be 
paid before dividends to owners of Common [[Stock]]. Preferred [[Stock]] 
owners usually [[DO]] not have [[voting rights]]. Preferred [[Stock]] holders carry 
additional rights above and beyond those conferred on a Common [[Stock]]. 

---

## Page 128

UW SOURCE: Eligibility Processing – Part 5 
Legal and Regulatory Processing 128 
Field Name Field Description 
• Limited Partnership 
A limited partnership consists of one or more general partners, who 
manage the business and assume legal debts and [[obligations]], and one 
or more limited partners who are liable only to the extent of their 
investments. These investors are not liable for legal action or debts the 
company may face. 
• American Depositary Receipt ([[ADR]]) 
A negotiable security issued by a US commercial bank that represents a 
specified number of [[shares]] in a foreign [[Stock]] that is traded in a US 
exchange. ADRs are denominated in US dollars. 
• [[Equity]] [[Derivatives]] 
 A hybrid security whose value is based on the performance of the 
underlying instrument. [[Equity]] Derivative trades in [[shares]]/ units. 
• Closed End Fund 
A type of fund having a limited number of [[shares]] listed on a [[Stock]] 
exchange. Closed funds redeem [[shares]] on a continuous basis. 
• Institutional Money Market Instrument (IMMI) 
A mutual fund whose investments are in high-yield money market 
instrumentssuch as federal [[securities]], CDs and commercial paper. Its 
intent is tomake such instruments, normally purchased in large 
denominations by [[Institutions]],available indirectly to individuals.  
• [[Equity]] Unit 
A security usually comprised of two other [[securities]] (the components) 
traded together; generally they consist of common [[Stock]] and a warrant. 
The unit can separate into the components immediately upon issuance or 
trade on its own until such time that the [[issuer]] decides to separate the unit 
into the components. The separation can be voluntary or mandatory. 
• Unit Investment Trust 
An investment company that offers a fixed, unmanaged portfolio, 
generally of stocks and [[Bonds]], as redeemable "units" to investors for a 
specific period of time. It is designed to provide capital appreciation 
and/or [[Dividend]] income. 
• Exchange Traded Funds 
A security that tracks an index, a commodity or a basket of assets like an 
index fund, but trades like a [[Stock]] on an exchange. ETFs experience 
price changes throughout the day as they are bought and sold. 

---

## Page 129

UW SOURCE: Eligibility Processing – Part 5 
Legal and Regulatory Processing 129 
Field Name Field Description 
• [[Warrants]] 
A warrant is a security that entitles its owner to buy a share of the 
issuing company's [[Stock]] at a predetermined price regardless of the 
[[Stock]]’s current market value.  [[Warrants]] are issued to enhance the future 
value of the [[Stock]] to the holder. 
• Depository [[shares]] 
A depository share represents ownership in the [[shares]] of a non-U.S. 
company that trades in U.S. financial markets. 
Like [[ADR]]'s Depository [[shares]] are created when [[shares]] of U.S. 
corporation are placed into a trust account or with a custodian bank.  The 
[[securities]] issued are termed to be Depository [[shares]]. 
• Rights 
A security giving stockholders [[Entitlement]] to purchase new [[shares]] 
issued by [[The Corporation]] at a predetermined price (normally less than 
the current market price) in proportion to the number of [[shares]] already 
owned. Rights are issued only for a short period of time, after which they 
expire. 
• Corporate Bond 
A debt obligation or IOU issued by public or private corporation that 
obligates the [[issuer]] to pay the bondholder principal plus interest. 
• Corporate VRDO  
Corporate Variable Rate Demand [[obligations]] (VRDO) is floating rate 
instrument. They pay interest monthly or quarterly based on a floating 
rate that is reset daily or weekly based on an index of short-term 
corporate debt rates. 
• Certificates of Deposit (CD) 
CDs are time deposit products that are FDIC insured and have flexible 
maturity periods ranging from seven days to 14 years. 
• Debt Unit 
A security usually comprised of two other [[securities]] (the components) 
traded together; generally they consist of corporate bond and a warrant. 
The unit can separate into the components immediately upon issuance 
or trade on its own until such time that the [[issuer]] decides to separate the 
unit into the components. The separation can be voluntary or mandatory.  

---

## Page 130

UW SOURCE: Eligibility Processing – Part 5 
Legal and Regulatory Processing 130 
Field Name Field Description 
• Corporate Insured Custodial Receipt 
Corporate [[Bonds]] are insured by insurance [[Companies]]. Corporate 
insured receipts represent the insured portion and are similar to the 
original corporate bond except for the security identification number. 
• Collateralized Mortgage Obligation(CMO) 
A type of mortgage backed security. 
• [[Asset]]-Backed Security ([[ABS]])  
A type of security backed by a pool of credit card receivables, home-
[[Equity]] loans, or other assets. 
• Government Security 
[[securities]] issued by any government agency.  
• Corporate Debt Derivative  
 A hybrid security whose value is based on the performance of the 
underlying instrument. Debt Derivative trades in the denominated 
currency. 
• Municipal Bond 
A debt obligation issued by municipality or local government agency. 
• Municipal VRDO 
Municipal Variable Rate Demand [[obligations]] (VRDO) is floating rate 
instruments with terms of as much as 40 years. They pay interest 
monthly or quarterly based on a floating rate that is reset daily or weekly 
based on an index of short-term municipal rates. 
• Municipal Note 
Shorter maturity period between issuance and redemption than 
amunicipal bond. Maturity period is one year or less.  
• Municipal Insured Custodial Receipt 
Municipal [[Bonds]] are insured by American Municipal Bond Assurance 
Corporation (AMBAC) or MBIA. A municipal insured custodial receipt 
represents the insured portion and is similar to the original municipal 
bond except for the security identification number. 
• Municipal Other Tax Exempt 
Municipal Other Tax Exempt is debt [[obligations]] that are issued by the 
state, county, city, or any other political sub- division. These [[Bonds]] are 
attractive to individual investors, since, as the name indicates, the 
government does not tax the interest received.

Maintaining ILOR / Riders 
This section provides the steps for all the tasks to maintain the ILOR ([[issuer]] Letter of Representations) /Riders. 
Legal user can perform the following maintenance tasks on the created ILOR/Riders. 
• Modify ILOR/Riders 
• Delete ILOR/Riders 
Modifying ILOR/Riders 
To modify ILOR/Riders: 
1. From the Eligibility menu, choose ILOR/Riders Processing->Search ILOR/Riders. 
The Search [[issuer]] Letter of Representations/Riders page appears. 
2. In the Search Criteria section, from the ILOR/Riders Status drop-down list, select Pending For Approval, 
Active, Draft or Matched, and click Search. 
The [[issuer]] Letter of Representation/Riders List page appears. 
3. Select an ILOR/Rider, and from the Action drop-down list, select Modify ILOR/Riders and click Go. 
The Modify [[issuer]] Letter of Representations/Riders page appears.

A security giving stockholders [[Entitlement]] to purchase new [[shares]] 
issued by [[The Corporation]] at a predetermined price (normally less than 
the current market price) in proportion to the number of [[shares]] already 
owned. Rights are issued only for a short period of time, after which they 
expire. 
• Corporate Bond 
A debt obligation or IOU issued by public or private corporation that 
obligates the [[issuer]] to pay the bondholder principal plus interest. 
• Corporate VRDO  
Corporate Variable Rate Demand [[obligations]] (VRDO) is floating rate 
instrument. They pay interest monthly or quarterly based on a floating 
rate that is reset daily or weekly based on an index of short-term 
corporate debt rates. 
• Certificates of Deposit (CD) 
CDs are time deposit products that are FDIC insured and have flexible 
maturity periods ranging from seven days to 14 years. 
• Debt Unit 
A security usually comprised of two other [[securities]] (the components) 
traded together; generally they consist of corporate bond and a warrant. 
The unit can separate into the components immediately upon issuance 
or trade on its own until such time that the [[issuer]] decides to separate the 
unit into the components. The separation can be voluntary or mandatory. 
 
• Corporate Insured Custodial Receipt 
Corporate [[Bonds]] are insured by insurance [[Companies]]. Corporate 
insured receipts represent the insured portion and are similar to the 
original corporate bond except for the security identification number. 
• Collateralized Mortgage Obligation(CMO) 
A type of mortgage backed security. 
• [[Asset]]-Backed Security ([[ABS]])  
A type of security backed by a pool of credit card receivables, home-

Field Name Field Description 
[[Equity]] loans, or other assets. 
• Government Security 
[[securities]] issued by any government agency.  
• Corporate Debt Derivative  
 A hybrid security whose value is based on the performance of the 
underlying instrument. Debt Derivative trades in the denominated 
currency. 
• Municipal Bond 
A debt obligation issued by municipality or local government agency. 
• Municipal VRDO 
Municipal Variable Rate Demand [[obligations]] (VRDO) is floating rate 
instruments with terms of as much as 40 years. They pay interest 
monthly or quarterly based on a floating rate that is reset daily or weekly 
based on an index of short-term municipal rates. 
• Municipal Note 
Shorter maturity period between issuance and redemption than 
amunicipal bond. Maturity period is one year or less.  
• Municipal Insured Custodial Receipt 
Municipal [[Bonds]] are insured by American Municipal Bond Assurance 
Corporation (AMBAC) or MBIA. A municipal insured custodial receipt 
represents the insured portion and is similar to the original municipal 
bond except for the security identification number. 
• Municipal Other Tax Exempt 
Municipal Other Tax Exempt is debt [[obligations]] that are issued by the 
state, county, city, or any other political sub- division. These [[Bonds]] are 
attractive to individual investors, since, as the name indicates, the 
government does not tax the interest received.

• Sub Issue Type Information Section 
Table 98: Sub Issue Type Information Section 
Field Name Field Description 
Sub Issue Type The type of Security. 
Valid values: 
• Common [[Stock]] 
A security that represents ownership in a corporation. Owners of Common 
[[Stock]] are on the bottom of the priority ladder of the ownership structure. 
• Preferred  [[Stock]] 
A class of ownership in a corporation with a stated [[Dividend]] that must be 
paid before dividends to owners of Common [[Stock]]. Preferred [[Stock]] 
owners usually [[DO]] not have [[voting rights]]. Preferred [[Stock]] holders carry 
additional rights above and beyond those conferred on a Common [[Stock]]. 
• Limited Partnership 
A limited partnership consists of one or more general partners, who 
manage the business and assume legal debts and [[obligations]], and one 
or more limited partners who are liable only to the extent of their 
investments. These investors are not liable for legal action or debts the 
company may face. 
• American Depositary Receipt ([[ADR]]) 
A negotiable security issued by a US commercial bank that represents a 
specified number of [[shares]] in a foreign [[Stock]] that is traded in a US 
exchange. ADRs are denominated in US dollars. 
• [[Equity]] [[Derivatives]] 

---

## Page 153

UW SOURCE: Eligibility Processing – Part 5 
Legal and Regulatory Processing 153 
Field Name Field Description 
 A hybrid security whose value is based on the performance of the 
underlying instrument. [[Equity]] Derivative trades in [[shares]]/ units. 
• Closed End Fund 
A type of fund having a limited number of [[shares]] listed on a [[Stock]] 
exchange. Closed funds redeem [[shares]] on a continuous basis. 
• Institutional Money Market Instrument (IMMI) 
A mutual fund whose investments are in high-yield money market 
instrumentssuch as federal [[securities]], CDs and commercial paper. Its 
intent is tomake such instruments, normally purchased in large 
denominations by [[Institutions]],available indirectly to individuals.  
• [[Equity]] Unit 
A security usually comprised of two other [[securities]] (the co mponents) 
traded together; generally they consist of common [[Stock]] and a warrant. 
The unit can separate into the components immediately upon issuance or 
trade on its own until such time that the [[issuer]] decides to separate the unit 
into the components. The separation can be voluntary or mandatory. 
• Unit Investment Trust 
An investment company that offers a fixed, unmanaged portfolio, 
generally of stocks and [[Bonds]], as redeemable "units" to investors for a 
specific period of time. It is designed to provide capital appreciation 
and/or [[Dividend]] income. 
• Exchange Traded Funds 
A security that tracks an index, a commodity or a basket of assets like an 
index fund, but trades like a [[Stock]] on an exchange. ETFs experience 
price changes throughout the day as they are bought and sold. 
• [[Warrants]] 
A warrant is a security that entitles its owner to buy a share of the 
issuing company's [[Stock]] at a predetermined price regardless of the 
[[Stock]]’s current market value.  [[Warrants]] are issued to enhance the future 
value of the [[Stock]] to the holder. 
• Depository [[shares]] 
A depository share represents ownership in the [[shares]] of a non-U.S. 
company that trades in U.S. financial markets.

Matching ILOR/Riders 
To match ILOR/Riders to an issue:

The Search [[issuer]] Letter of Representation/Riders page appears.

3. In the [[issuer]] Letter of Representations List section, select an ILOR/Rider and from the Action drop-down 
list, select Match Issue and click Go. 
The Select Issue for ILOR/Riders Matching page appears.

The Match ILOR/Riders to Issue page appears.

Matching Issue to ILOR/Riders 
To match an issue to ILOR/Riders: 
1. From the Eligibility menu, choose Search Issue. 
2. Enter the search criteria and click Search. 
3. In the Issue List section, select the desired issue. 
4. From the Action drop-down list, select Match ILOR/Riders and click Go.  
The Select ILOR/Riders for Issue Matching page appears.

Table 110: [[issuer]] Information Section 
Field Name Field Description 
[[issuer]] A corporate or municipality offering [[securities]] for sale to investors. Examples 
include corporations, investment trusts, and government entities. 
[[issuer]] Type The type of [[issuer]]. 
Valid values: 
• Municipal 
Select Municipal when a state or local government entity is involved in 
issuing [[securities]] to raise funds through a public or private offering of 
debt. 
• Corporate 
Select Corporate when a corporate is involved in issuing [[securities]] to 
raise funds for the corporate venture or other corporate activities. 
[[issuer]] Short Name The unique short name given to the [[issuer]] based on ISO 18773 standard 
Country of Incorporation The country in which the [[issuer]] company is incorporated. 
State of Incorporation The state in which the [[issuer]] company is incorporated. 
Non US State The name of the non-US state if the officer is non-US. 
Country of Origin The country of domicile of the [[issuer]]. 
State of Origin The state of domicile of the [[issuer]]. 
Non US State The name of the non-US state if the officer is non-US. 
Federal Tax 
Identification Number 
The Employer Identification Number (EIN) that uniquely identifies the business 
entity 
 
Sub Issue Type Information Section 
Table 111: Sub Issue Type Information Section 
Field Name Field Description 
Sub Issue Type The type of Security. 
Valid values: 
• Common [[Stock]] 
A security that represents ownership in a corporation. Owners of Common 
[[Stock]] are on the bottom of the priority ladder of the ownership structure. 
• Preferred  [[Stock]] 

---

## Page 170

UW SOURCE: Eligibility Processing – Part 5 
Legal and Regulatory Processing 170 
Field Name Field Description 
A class of ownership in a corporation with a stated [[Dividend]] that must be 
paid before dividends to owners of Common [[Stock]]. Preferred [[Stock]] 
owners usually [[DO]] not have [[voting rights]]. Preferred [[Stock]] holders carry 
additional rights above and beyond those conferred on a Common [[Stock]]. 
• Limited Partnership 
A limited partnership consists of one or more general partners, who 
manage the business and assume legal debts and [[obligations]], and one 
or more limited partners who are liable only to the extent of their 
investments. These investors are not liable for legal action or debts the 
company may face. 
• American Depositary Receipt ([[ADR]]) 
A negotiable security issued by a US commercial bank that represents a 
specified number of [[shares]] in a foreign [[Stock]] that is traded in a US 
exchange. ADRs are denominated in US dollars. 
• [[Equity]] [[Derivatives]] 
 A hybrid security whose value is based on the performance of the 
underlying instrument. [[Equity]] Derivative trades in [[shares]]/ units. 
• Closed End Fund 
A type of fund having a limited number of [[shares]] listed on a [[Stock]] 
exchange. Closed funds redeem [[shares]] on a continuous basis. 
• Institutional Money Market Instrument (IMMI) 
A mutual fund whose investments are in high-yield money market 
instrumentssuch as federal [[securities]], CDs and commercial paper. Its 
intent is tomake such instruments, normally purchased in large 
denominations by [[Institutions]],available indirectly to individuals.  
• [[Equity]] Unit 
A security usually comprised of two other [[securities]] (the components) 
traded together; generally they consist of common [[Stock]] and a warrant. 
The unit can separate into the components immediately upon issuance or 
trade on its own until such time that the [[issuer]] decides to separate the unit 
into the components. The separation can be voluntary or mandatory. 
• Unit Investment Trust 
An investment company that offers a fixed, unmanaged portfolio, 
generally of stocks and [[Bonds]], as redeemable "units" to investors for a 
specific period of time. It is designed to provide capital appreciation 
and/or [[Dividend]] income. 

---

## Page 171

UW SOURCE: Eligibility Processing – Part 5 
Legal and Regulatory Processing 171 
Field Name Field Description 
• Exchange Traded Funds 
A security that tracks an index, a commodity or a basket of assets like an 
index fund, but trades like a [[Stock]] on an exchange. ETFs experience 
price changes throughout the day as they are bought and sold. 
• [[Warrants]] 
A warrant is a security that entitles its owner to buy a share of the 
issuing company's [[Stock]] at a predetermined price regardless of the 
[[Stock]]’s current market value.  [[Warrants]] are issued to enhance the future 
value of the [[Stock]] to the holder. 
• Depository [[shares]] 
A depository share represents ownership in the [[shares]] of a non-U.S. 
company that trades in U.S. financial markets. 
Like [[ADR]]'s Depository [[shares]] are created when [[shares]] of U.S. 
corporation are placed into a trust account or with a custodian bank.  The 
[[securities]] issued are termed to be Depository [[shares]]. 
• Rights 
A security giving stockholders [[Entitlement]] to purchase new [[shares]] 
issued by [[The Corporation]] at a predetermined price (normally less than 
the current market price) in proportion to the number of [[shares]] already 
owned. Rights are issued only for a short period of time, after which they 
expire. 
• Corporate Bond 
A debt obligation or IOU issued by public or private corporation that 
obligates the [[issuer]] to pay the bondholder principal plus interest. 
• Corporate VRDO  
Corporate Variable Rate Demand [[obligations]] (VRDO) is floating rate 
instrument. They pay interest monthly or quarterly based on a floating 
rate that is reset daily or weekly based on an index of short-term 
corporate debt rates. 
• Certificates of Deposit (CD) 
CDs are time deposit products that are FDIC insured and have flexible 
maturity periods ranging from seven days to 14 years. 
• Debt Unit 

---

## Page 172

UW SOURCE: Eligibility Processing – Part 5 
Legal and Regulatory Processing 172 
Field Name Field Description 
A security usually comprised of two other [[securities]] (the components) 
traded together; generally they consist of corporate bond and a warrant. 
The unit can separate into the components immediately upon issuance 
or trade on its own until such time that the [[issuer]] decides to separate the 
unit into the components. The separation can be voluntary or mandatory.  
• Corporate Insured Custodial Receipt 
Corporate [[Bonds]] are insured by insurance [[Companies]]. Corporate 
insured receipts represent the insured portion and are similar to the 
original corporate bond except for the security identification number. 
• Collateralized Mortgage Obligation(CMO) 
A type of mortgage backed security. 
• [[Asset]]-Backed Security ([[ABS]])  
A type of security backed by a pool of credit card receivables, home-
[[Equity]] loans, or other assets. 
• Government Security 
[[securities]] issued by any government agency.  
• Corporate Debt Derivative  
 A hybrid security whose value is based on the performance of the 
underlying instrument. Debt Derivative trades in the denominated 
currency. 
• Municipal Bond 
A debt obligation issued by municipality or local government agency. 
• Municipal VRDO 
Municipal Variable Rate Demand [[obligations]] (VRDO) is floating rate 
instruments with terms of as much as 40 years. They pay interest 
monthly or quarterly based on a floating rate that is reset daily or weekly 
based on an index of short-term municipal rates. 
• Municipal Note 
Shorter maturity period between issuance and redemption than 
amunicipal bond. Maturity period is one year or less.  
• Municipal Insured Custodial Receipt 
Municipal [[Bonds]] are insured by American Municipal Bond Assurance 
Corporation (AMBAC) or MBIA. A municipal insured custodial receipt 
represents the insured portion and is similar to the original municipal 
bond except for the security identification number.

UW SOURCE: Eligibility Processing – Part 5 
Legal and Regulatory Processing 173 
Field Name Field Description 
• Municipal Other Tax Exempt 
Municipal Other Tax Exempt is debt [[obligations]] that are issued by the 
state, county, city, or any other political sub- division. These [[Bonds]] are 
attractive to individual investors, si nce, as the name indicates, the 
government does not tax the interest received.

Unmatching ILOR/Riders 
If an ILOR/Rider is matched with the incorrect issue or vice versa, UW SOURCE allows the [[DTC]] legal user to 
unmatch a matched ILOR/Riders or issue. 
To unmatch the issue to ILOR/Riders: 
1. From the Eligibility menu, choose Search Issue. 
2. Enter the search criteria and click Search. 
3. In the Issue List section, select an issue and from the Action drop-down list, select Unmatch ILOR/Riders 
and click Go. 
The Unmatch Issue and ILOR/Riders page appears. 
 
Figure 34: Unmatch Issue and ILOR/Riders Page 
4. Click Submit.
