## Page 177

UW SOURCE: Eligibility Processing – Part 5 
Legal and Regulatory Processing 177 
Unmatching ILOR/Riders and an Issue 
To unmatch ILOR/Riders and an issue: 
1. From the Eligibility menu, choose ILOR/Riders Processing->Search ILOR/Riders. 
The Search [[issuer]] Letter of Representation /Riders page appears. 
2. In the Search  Criteria section, from the ILOR/Riders Status drop- down list, select Matched and click 
Search. 
3. In the [[issuer]] Letter of Representations List section, select an ILOR. 
4. From the Action drop-down list, select Unmatch Issue and click Go. 
The Unmatch ILOR/Riders page appears. 
 
Figure 35: Unmatch ILOR/Riders Page 
5. Click Submit.

• Final 
Indicates that the final version of the ILOR has been received. 
• Signed Fax copy 
Indicates that the signed fax copy of the ILOR has been received. 
ILOR/Rider Status Valid values: 
• Pending for approval 
Indicates that the ILOR/Riders created, is pending for approval by the 
Eligibility User. 
• Active 
Indicates that a valid and approved ILOR/Rider exists for the [[issuer]]. 
• Inactive 
Indicates that the ILOR/Riders were deleted for the [[issuer]] and the details 
are available for query purposes. 
• Rejected 
Indicates that the ILOR/Riders creation was rejected during approval 
process. 
• Matched 
Indicates that the ILOR/Riders are matched with an Issue of the [[issuer]]. 
Information Type Valid values: 
• ILOR only 
Only an ILOR is required to be matched for the Issue 
• Riders only 
Only Riders are required for the Issue and ILOR is not required as the 
Issue is matched with BLOR 
• ILOR and Riders 
Both ILOR as well as Riders are required to be matched with the Issue.

6. EXCHANGE LETTER PROCESSING 
This function is to receive and process Exchange Letters for 144A issues when the [[securities]] in the issue are 
exchanged from 144A to Non-144A. 
Creating an Exchange Letter 
To create an exchange letter: 
1. From the Eligibility menu, choose Exchange Letter Processing->Create Exchange Letter. 
The Create Exchange Letter page appears. 
 
Figure 36: Create Exchange Letter Page 
  


---

## Page 187

UW SOURCE: Eligibility Processing – Part 5 
Exchange Letter Processing 187 
 
2. In the [[issuer]] Information section, enter the [[issuer]] [[ID]].  
The [[issuer]] Information section is automatically populated with the corresponding [[issuer]] information. 
3. From the Exchange Letter Type drop-down list, select the desired exchange letter type. 
4. In the Document Date field, select the desired document date (Optional).  
5. Enter the Received Date, Received User and Internal Comments (Optional).  
6. In the Exchange Letter-Security List, add the security [[ID]] (Optional). 
7. From the Approve drop-down list, select the approval status (Optional). 
8. In the Approval Information section, enter Internal Comments (Optional). 
9. Attach document information (Optional). 
• In the Document Name field, click Attach.  
A pop-up appears. 
• Click Browse and select the required document.  
• Click Submit. 
10. Click Submit.

Searching for an Issue for Eligibility Review 
To search for an issue for eligibility review: 
1. From the Eligibility menu, click Search Issue. 
Note: It is mandatory to enter at least one of the following fields to perform search in this page 
• [[issuer]] type (or) 
• Issue eligible status (If Issue Eligible Status is ‘Approved’ or ‘Successful systemic eligibility review’ then 
Expected Closing Date must be specified) 
2. In the Issue related search criteria section, specify the required fields. 
Note: To perform an advanced search using Eligibility Processing Statuses, click Expand Search. 
3. Select the relevant status in the Eligibility Processing Statuses Search Criteria section and click Search.

OFAC Compliance Status Valid values: 
• Pending Online Scan 
Indicates that the information has been sent for Online OFAC Scan and 
the scan results are expected. 
• Pending Detailed Scan 
Indicates that the Online OFAC Scan has identified a ‘Potential Hit’. As 
a result, the information is pending for Batch OFAC Scan for detailed 
scan results. 
• No Hit 
Indicates that the information has been scanned and there ar e no 
matching Stop Descriptor(s). 
• Override 
Indicates that a newer version of the information is available and the 
information scanned earlier or which was in process is no longer valid. 
In this case, irrespective of the scan results, the status of the old 
information will be set to Overridden. 
• Potential Hit 
Indicates that the information has been scanned and flagged with one 
or more matching Stop Descriptor(s). 
• False Positive 
Indicates that the information was flagged by the system as Potential Hit 
or True Hit but checked by Eligibility Supervisor and resolved as OFAC 
compliant. 
• True Hit

---

## Page 215

UW SOURCE: Eligibility Processing – Part 5 
Eligibility QA Processing 215 
Field Name Field Description 
structure. 
• Preferred  [[Stock]] 
A class of ownership in a corporation with a stated [[Dividend]] that must 
be paid before dividends to owners of Common [[Stock]]. Preferred [[Stock]] 
owners usually [[DO]] not have [[voting rights]]. Preferred [[Stock]] holders carry 
additional rights above and beyond those conferred on a Common 
[[Stock]]. 
• Limited Partnership 
A limited partnership consists of one or more general partners, who 
manage the business and assume legal debts and [[obligations]], and 
one or more limited partners who are liable only to the extent of their 
investments. These investors are not liable for legal action or debts the 
company may face. 
• American Depositary Receipt ([[ADR]]) 
A negotiable security issued by a US commercial bank that represents 
a specified number of [[shares]] in a foreign [[Stock]] that is traded in a US 
exchange. ADRs are denominated in US dollars. 
• [[Equity]] [[Derivatives]] 
 A hybrid security whose value is based on the performance of the 
underlying instrument. [[Equity]] Derivative trades in [[shares]]/ units. 
• Closed End Fund 
A type of fund having a limited number of [[shares]] listed on a [[Stock]] 
exchange. Closed funds redeem [[shares]] on a continuous basis. 
• Institutional Money Market Instrument (IMMI) 
A mutual fund whose investments are in high-yield money market 
instrumentssuch as federal [[securities]], CDs and commercial paper. Its 
intent is tomake such instruments, normally purchased in large 
denominations by [[Institutions]],available indirectly to individuals.  
• [[Equity]] Unit 
A security usually comprised of two other [[securities]] (the components) 
traded together; generally they consist of common [[Stock]] and a warrant. 
The unit can separate into the components immediately upon issuance 
or trade on its own until such time that the [[issuer]] decides to separate 
the unit into the components. The separation can be voluntary or 
mandatory. 
• Unit Investment Trust 

---

## Page 216

UW SOURCE: Eligibility Processing – Part 5 
Eligibility QA Processing 216 
Field Name Field Description 
An investment company that offers a fixed, unmanaged portfolio, 
generally of stocks and [[Bonds]], as redeemable "units" to investors for a 
specific period of time. It is designed to provide capital appreciation 
and/or [[Dividend]] income. 
• Exchange Traded Funds 
A security that tracks an index, a commodity or a basket of assets like 
an index fund, but trades like a [[Stock]] on an exchange. ETFs 
experience price changes throughout the day as they are bought and 
sold. 
• [[Warrants]] 
A warrant is a security that entitles its owner to buy a share of the 
issuing company's [[Stock]] at a predetermined price regardless of the 
[[Stock]]’s current market value.  [[Warrants]] are issued to enhance the 
future value of the [[Stock]] to the holder. 
• Depository [[shares]] 
A depository share represents ownership in the [[shares]] of a non-U.S. 
company that trades in U.S. financial markets. 
Like [[ADR]]'s Depository [[shares]] are created when [[shares]] of U.S. 
corporation are placed into a trust account or with a custodian bank.  
The [[securities]] issued are termed to be Depository [[shares]]. 
• Rights 
A security giving stockholders [[Entitlement]] to purchase new [[shares]] 
issued by [[The Corporation]] at a predetermined price (normally less than 
the current market price) in proportion to the number of [[shares]] already 
owned. Rights are issued only for a short period of time, after which 
they expire. 
• Corporate Bond 
A debt obligation or IOU issued by public or private corporation that 
obligates the [[issuer]] to pay the bondholder principal plus interest. 
• Corporate VRDO  
Corporate Variable Rate Demand [[obligations]] (VRDO) is floating rate 
instrument. They pay interest monthly or quarterly based on a floating 
rate that is reset daily or weekly based on an index of short-term 
corporate debt rates. 

---

## Page 217

UW SOURCE: Eligibility Processing – Part 5 
Eligibility QA Processing 217 
Field Name Field Description 
• Certificates of Deposit (CD) 
CDs are time deposit products that are FDIC insured and have flexible 
maturity periods ranging from seven days to 14 years. 
• Debt Unit 
A security usually comprised of two other [[securities]] (the components) 
traded together; generally they consist of corporate bond and a 
warrant. The unit can separate into the components immediately upon 
issuance or trade on its own until such time that the [[issuer]] decides to 
separate the unit into the components. The separation can be 
voluntary or mandatory.  
• Corporate Insured Custodial Receipt 
Corporate [[Bonds]] are insured by insurance [[Companies]]. Corporate 
insured receipts represent the insured portion and are similar to the 
original corporate bond except for the security identification number. 
• Collateralized Mortgage Obligation(CMO) 
A type of mortgage backed security. 
• [[Asset]]-Backed Security ([[ABS]])  
A type of security backed by a pool of credit card receivables, home-
[[Equity]] loans, or other assets. 
• Government Security 
[[securities]] issued by any government agency.  
• Corporate Debt Derivative  
 A hybrid security whose value is based on the performance of the 
underlying instrument. Debt Derivative trades in the denominated 
currency. 
• Municipal Bond 
A debt obligation issued by municipality or local government agency. 
• Municipal VRDO 
Municipal Variable Rate Demand [[obligations]] (VRDO) is floating rate 
instruments with terms of as much as 40 years. They pay interest 
monthly or quarterly based on a floating rate that is reset daily or 
weekly based on an index of short-term municipal rates. 
• Municipal Note 
Shorter maturity period between issuance and redemption than

Field Name Field Description 
amunicipal bond. Maturity period is one year or less.  
• Municipal Insured Custodial Receipt 
Municipal [[Bonds]] are insured by American Municipal Bond Assurance 
Corporation (AMBAC) or MBIA. A municipal insured custodial receipt 
represents the insured portion and is similar to the original municipal 
bond except for the security identification number. 
• Municipal Other Tax Exempt 
Municipal Other Tax Exempt is debt [[obligations]] that are issued by the 
state, county, city, or any other political sub- division. These [[Bonds]] are 
attractive to individual investors, since, as the name indicates, the 
government does not tax the interest received.

Indicates that a Dummy Agent [[ID]] is present and an Agent Confirmation has 
not been performed. 
• Pending For [[OA]] Check Completion 
Indicates that an Operational Arrangement Check is not yet successful for 
the agent. [[OA]] check should be successful to start Agent confirmation 
process. 
• Pending For Agent [[ID]] Confirmation 
Indicates that an issue is pending with an agent for Agent [[ID]] Confirmation. 
• Pending for required information 
Indicates that the Agent [[ID]] Confirmation is successful but pending for 
required issue information that needs to be confirmed by the agent. 
• Rejected 
Indicates that an agent has rejected the issue stating that he is not the Agent 
for the Issue. 
• Pending for Issue Details Confirmation 
Indicates that an issue is pending with an agent for Issue Details 
Confirmation. 
• Confirmed 
Indicates that an agent has confirmed the Issue details. 
• Confirmed With Update 
Indicates that an agent has confirmed the Issue details with certain updates 
to the information. 
• Not Applicable 
Indicates that the TA Confirmation Check Process is not applicable for the 
issue. 
• Internal Agent [[ID]] no Confirmation required 
Indicates that an Internal Agent [[ID]] is present and an Agent Confirmation is 
not required.

## Page 224

UW SOURCE: Eligibility Processing – Part 5 
Eligibility QA Processing 224 
Entering Eligibility QA Information 
To enter eligibility QA information: 
1. In the Search Issue for Eligibility Review page, specify the search criteria and click Search. 
2. From the Action drop-down list, select Enter Eligibility QA Information and click Go. 
The Enter Eligibility QA Information page appears. 
 
Figure 45: Enter Eligibility QA Information Page 
3. Select the applicable sub issue type and click Submit 
4. In the Issue Level Eligibility QA section, specify the desired values. 
5. Add [[underwriter]] details (Optional). 
6. In the Security List section, enter the desired values and click Submit.  
Enter Eligibility QA Information Page 
The Enter Eligibility QA Information page enables you to enter the eligibility QA information.  
The sections in this page are: 
• Issue Information 
• Document Used for Eligibility QA 
• Sub Issue Type Information 
• Issue Level Eligibility QA Fields 
• [[underwriter]] List 
• Security List 
Issue Information Section 
This section displays the issue identification information. This information is view only and cannot be edited.

• Certificate  
A [[Stock]] certificate (also known as certificate of [[Stock]] or share certificate) is a legal 
document that certifies ownership of a specific number of [[Stock]] [[shares]] (or fractions

• Indenture 
A formal agreement between the [[issuer]] of [[Bonds]] and the bondholders about the 
terms of the debt. 
• Offering Memorandum 
A legal document stating the objectives, risks and terms of investment involved with a 
[[Private Placement]]. This includes items such as the financial statements, 
management biographies, detailed description of the business, etc. An offering 
memorandum serves to provide buyers with information on the offering and to protect 
the sellers from the liability associated with selling unregistered [[securities]]. 
• Official Statement 
A shortened registration statement required by the [[securities]] and Exchange 
Commission on municipal  debt issues  
• Ordinance 
A law made by a non-sovereign body such as a city council or a colony. 
• Placement Agreement 
The sale of [[securities]] directly to private persons, and/or institutional investors, 
outside a [[public offering]]. Unlike a [[public offering]], a [[Private Placement]] does not have 
to be registered with the [[SEC]]. 
• [[Private Placement]] Memorandum 
[[Private Placement]] Memorandum is the document that describes the sale of an entire 
issue of [[securities]] to a small group of investors. 
• Prospectus 
A document that provides details about a new offering of [[securities]] for sale to the 
public. It gives a detailed financial background of the issuing company, how the 
proceeds of the [[securities]] will be used, and other pertinent information investors will

Name

Sub 
Issue 
Type 
The type of Security. 
Valid values: 
• Common [[Stock]] 
A security that represents ownership in a corporation. Owners of Common [[Stock]] are on the 
bottom of the priority ladder of the ownership structure. 
• Preferred  [[Stock]] 
A class of ownership in a corporation with a stated [[Dividend]] that must be paid before 
dividends to owners of Common [[Stock]]. Preferred [[Stock]] owners usually [[DO]] not have voting 
rights. Preferred [[Stock]] holders carry additional rights above and beyond those conferred on 
a Common [[Stock]].

## Page 228

UW SOURCE: Eligibility Processing – Part 5 
Eligibility QA Processing 228 
Field 
Name 
Field Description 
• Limited Partnership 
A limited partnership consists of one or more general partners, who manage the business 
and assume legal debts and [[obligations]], and one or more limited partners who are liable 
only to the extent of their investments. These investors are not liable for legal action or 
debts the company may face. 
• American Depositary Receipt ([[ADR]]) 
A negotiable security issued by a US commercial bank that represents a specified number 
of [[shares]] in a foreign [[Stock]] that is traded in a US exchange. ADRs are denominated in US 
dollars. 
• [[Equity]] [[Derivatives]] 
 A hybrid security whose value is based on the performance of the underlying instrument. 
[[Equity]] Derivative trades in [[shares]]/ units. 
• Closed End Fund 
A type of fund having a limited number of [[shares]] listed on a [[Stock Exchange]]. Closed funds 
redeem [[shares]] on a continuous basis. 
• Institutional Money Market Instrument (IMMI) 
A mutual fund whose investments are in high-yield money market instrumentssuch as 
federal [[securities]], CDs and commercial paper. Its intent is tomake such instruments, 
normally purchased in large denominations by [[Institutions]],available indirectly to individuals.  
• [[Equity]] Unit 
A security usually comprised of two other [[securities]] (the components) traded together; 
generally they consist of common [[Stock]] and a warrant. The unit can separate into the 
components immediately upon issuance or trade on its own until such time that the [[issuer]] 
decides to separate the unit into the components. The separation can be voluntary or 
mandatory. 
• Unit Investment Trust 
An investment company that offers a fixed, unmanaged portfolio, generally of stocks and 
[[Bonds]], as redeemable "units" to investors for a specific period of time. It is designed to 
provide capital appreciation and/or [[Dividend]] income. 
• Exchange Traded Funds 
A security that tracks an index, a commodity or a basket of assets like an index fund, but 
trades like a [[Stock]] on an exchange. ETFs experience price changes throughout the day as 
they are bought and sold. 
• [[Warrants]] 

---

## Page 229

UW SOURCE: Eligibility Processing – Part 5 
Eligibility QA Processing 229 
Field 
Name 
Field Description 
A warrant is a security that entitles its owner to buy a share of the issuing company's [[Stock]] 
at a predetermined price regardless of the [[Stock]]’s current market value.  [[Warrants]] are 
issued to enhance the future value of the [[Stock]] to the holder. 
• Depository [[shares]] 
A depository share represents ownership in the [[shares]] of a non-U.S. company that trades 
in U.S. financial markets. 
Like [[ADR]]'s Depository [[shares]] are created when [[shares]] of U.S. corporation are placed into 
a trust account or with a custodian bank.  The [[securities]] issued are termed to be Depository 
[[shares]]. 
• Rights 
A security giving stockholders [[Entitlement]] to purchase new [[shares]] issued by the 
corporation at a predetermined price (normally less than the current market price) in 
proportion to the number of [[shares]] already owned. Rights are issued only for a short period 
of time, after which they expire. 
• Corporate Bond 
A debt obligation or IOU issued by public or private corporation that obligates the [[issuer]] to 
pay the bondholder principal plus interest. 
• Corporate VRDO  
Corporate Variable Rate Demand [[obligations]] (VRDO) is floating rate instrument. They pay 
interest monthly or quarterly based on a floating rate that is reset daily or weekly based on 
an index of short-term corporate debt rates. 
• Certificates of Deposit (CD) 
CDs are time deposit products that are FDIC insured and have flexible maturity periods 
ranging from seven days to 14 years. 
• Debt Unit 
A security usually comprised of two other [[securities]] (the components) traded together; 
generally they consist of corporate bond and a warrant. The unit can separate into the 
components immediately upon issuance or trade on its own until such time that the [[issuer]] 
decides to separate the unit into the components. The separation can be voluntary or 
mandatory.  
• Corporate Insured Custodial Receipt 

---

## Page 230

UW SOURCE: Eligibility Processing – Part 5 
Eligibility QA Processing 230 
Field 
Name 
Field Description 
Corporate [[Bonds]] are insured by insurance [[Companies]]. Corporate insured receipts 
represent the insured portion and are similar to the original corporate bond except for the 
security identification number. 
• Collateralized Mortgage Obligation(CMO) 
A type of mortgage backed security. 
• [[Asset]]-Backed Security ([[ABS]])  
A type of security backed by a pool of credit card receivables, home-[[Equity]] loans, or other 
assets. 
• Government Security 
[[securities]] issued by any government agency.  
• Corporate Debt Derivative  
 A hybrid security whose value is based on the performance of the underlying instrument. 
Debt Derivative trades in the denominated currency. 
• Municipal Bond 
A debt obligation issued by municipality or local government agency. 
• Municipal VRDO 
Municipal Variable Rate Demand [[obligations]] (VRDO) is floating rate instruments with terms 
of as much as 40 years. They pay interest monthly or quarterly based on a floating rate that 
is reset daily or weekly based on an index of short-term municipal rates. 
• Municipal Note 
Shorter maturity period between issuance and redemption than amunicipal bond. Maturity 
period is one year or less.  
• Municipal Insured Custodial Receipt 
Municipal [[Bonds]] are insured by American Municipal Bond Assurance Corporation (AMBAC) 
or MBIA. A municipal insured custodial receipt represents the insured portion and is similar 
to the original municipal bond except for the security identification number. 
• Municipal Other Tax Exempt 
Municipal Other Tax Exempt is debt [[obligations]] that are issued by the state, county, city, or 
any other political sub-division. These [[Bonds]] are attractive to individual investors, since, as 
the name indicates, the government does not tax the interest received.

## Page 231

UW SOURCE: Eligibility Processing – Part 5 
Eligibility QA Processing 231 
Field Name Field Description 
Call Type Indicates the type of execution of the call feature. 
Valid values:  
• Lottery 
The lottery payout structure involves a method of random draws. 
• [[Pro-Rata]] 
Used to describe a proportionate allocation. 
Callable This field is used to indicate if the issue is subject to an early redemption prior to maturity. Call 
feature can be set up only if this flag is turned on. 
Certificate 
Type 
The form of holding of the security 
Valid values: 
• Book Entry Only 
Book entry is a system of tracking ownership of [[securities]] where no certificate is 
given to investors. In the case of [[book-entry]]-only ([[BEO]]) issues, while investors [[DO]] 
not receive certificates, a custodian holds one or more global certificates. 
Dematerialized [[securities]], in contrast are ones in which no certificates exist 
(instead, the security [[issuer]] or its agent keeps records, usually electronically, of 
who holds outstanding [[securities]]).  
• Registered (Certificated) 
The name given to [[securities]] whereby ownership is registered with the issuing 
company or their agent. Registered form means the issuing firm keeps records of a 
security's owner and mails out payments to him/her. Most [[securities]] issued today 
are in registered form. 
• Bearer   
A security not registered in the books of issuing corporation but that is payable to 
its bearer (the person possessing it). Bearer form means the security is traded 
without any record of ownership, so physical possession of the security is the sole 
evidence of ownership.   
• Interchangeable 
These [[securities]] are issued initially in bearer or registered form and can be 
converted from bearer to registered form. However, they cannot be converted back 
to bearer form. 
• Dematerialized  
[[securities]] no longer need to be in certificate form. They can be registered and 
transferred electronically. 
Income 
Source 
Country 
The country where the income is generated 
Valid value is ISO Country List

## Page 234

UW SOURCE: Eligibility Processing – Part 5 
Eligibility QA Processing 234 
Resolving Eligibility QA Information 
To resolve eligibility QA mismatches: 
1. Search the required issue using the Search Issue for Eligibility Review page as explained in the Search 
Issue section of this document. 
2. From the Issue List, select Action item Resolve Eligibility QA Mismatches and click Go. 
The Resolve QA Mismatches – Issue Level page appears.  
 
Figure 46: Resolve QA Mismatches – Issue Level 
3. In the Resolve Eligibility QA Mismatches - Issue Level page, resolve the QA mismatches, if any and click 
Save. 
4. Select the security, and in the Security List, click Resolve. 
The Resolve Security Level QA Mismatches page appears.  


---

## Page 235

UW SOURCE: Eligibility Processing – Part 5 
Eligibility QA Processing 235 
 
Figure 47: Resolve Security Level QA Mismatches Page 
5. Resolve the QA mismatches, if any and click Save. 
6. Select the [[underwriter]] and click Resolve. 
The Resolve Security Level QA Mismatches page appears. 
7. Resolve the QA mismatches and click Save. 
8. Repeat the above process for each issue, security and [[underwriter]] until all mismatches are resolved and 
saved. 
9. In the Resolve [[underwriter]] Level QA Mismatches page, click Back to Security level information,  and 
in the Resolve Security Level QA Mismatches page, click Back to Issue level information. 
10. In the Resolve Eligibility QA Mismatches - Issue Level page, click Submit. 
The message, “QA mismatches resolved successfully”, is displayed. 
Resolve Eligibility QA Mismatches – Issue Level Page 
The Resolve Eligibility QA Mismatches – Issue Level page enables you to resolve issue level QA mismatches. 
This page displays only the mismatched fields for resolution. 
The sections available on this page are: 
• Issue Information

• Municipal Note 
Shorter maturity period between issuance and redemption than amunicipal bond. Maturity 
period is one year or less.

Municipal [[Bonds]] are insured by American Municipal Bond Assurance Corporation (AMBAC)

• Common [[Stock]] 
A security that represents ownership in a corporation. Owners of Common [[Stock]] are on 
the bottom of the priority ladder of the ownership structure. 
• Preferred  [[Stock]] 
A class of ownership in a corporation with a stated [[Dividend]] that must be paid before 
dividends to owners of Common [[Stock]]. Preferred [[Stock]] owners usually [[DO]] not have 
[[voting rights]]. Preferred [[Stock]] holders carry additional rights above and beyond those 
conferred on a Common [[Stock]]. 
• Limited Partnership 
A limited partnership consists of one or more general partners, who manage the 
business and assume legal debts and [[obligations]], and one or more limited partners 
who are liable only to the extent of their investments. These investors are not liable for 
legal action or debts the company may face. 
• American Depositary Receipt ([[ADR]]) 
A negotiable security issued by a US commercial bank that represents a specified 
number of [[shares]] in a foreign [[Stock]] that is traded in a US exchange. ADRs are 
denominated in US dollars. 
• [[Equity]] [[Derivatives]] 
 A hybrid security whose value is based on the performance of the underlying 
instrument. [[Equity]] Derivative trades in [[shares]]/ units. 
• Closed End Fund 
A type of fund having a limited number of [[shares]] listed on a [[Stock Exchange]]. Closed 
funds redeem [[shares]] on a continuous basis. 
• Institutional Money Market Instrument (IMMI) 
A mutual fund whose investments are in high-yield money market instrumentssuch as 
federal [[securities]], CDs and commercial paper. Its intent is tomake such instruments, 
normally purchased in large denominations by [[Institutions]],available indirectly to 
individuals.  
• [[Equity]] Unit 
A security usually comprised of two other [[securities]] (the components) traded together; 
generally they consist of common [[Stock]] and a warrant. The unit can separate into the

components immediately upon issuance or trade on its own until su ch time that the

An investment company that offers a fixed, unmanaged portfolio, generally of stocks 
and [[Bonds]], as redeemable "units" to investors for a specific period of time. It is 
designed to provide capital appreciation and/or [[Dividend]] income. 
• Exchange Traded Funds 
A security that tracks an index, a commodity or a basket of assets like an index fund, 
but trades like a [[Stock]] on an exchange. ETFs experience price changes throughout 
the day as they are bought and sold. 
• [[Warrants]] 
A warrant is a security that entitles its owner to buy a share of the issuing company's

[[Warrants]] are issued to enhance the future value of the [[Stock]] to the holder. 
• Depository [[shares]] 
A depository share represents ownership in the [[shares]] of a non-U.S. company that 
trades in U.S. financial markets.

into a trust account or with a custodian bank.  The [[securities]] issued are termed to be 
Depository [[shares]]. 
• Rights 
A security giving stockholders [[Entitlement]] to purchase new [[shares]] issued by the 
corporation at a predetermined price (normally less than the current market price) in 
proportion to the number of [[shares]] already owned. Rights are issued only for a short 
period of time, after which they expire.

[[issuer]] to pay the bondholder principal plus interest.

## Page 252

UW SOURCE: Eligibility Processing – Part 5 
Foreign Legal Opinion Compliance 252 
8. FOREIGN LEGAL OPINION COMPLIANCE 
Need for Foreign Legal Opinion Review 
The Foreign Legal Opinion (FLO) review is required for all foreign issues. An issue is considered “foreign” 
whenever the [[issuer]]’s or issue’s country of origin is non-US. 
When an [[underwriter]] requests that non-US [[securities]] be made eligible at [[DTC]], a legal opinion that outlines the 
requirements for [[DTC]] eligibility is prepared by an attorney. The attorney who prepares this outline must be 
qualified to practice law in the [[issuer]]’s country of incorporation with respect to the relevant laws of that country.   
The legal opinion must be on file with the [[DTC]] General Counsel’s office for all foreign issues. 
The foreign legal opinion review list is used to check whether all country information for the [[issuer]] and issue are 
compliant with respect to foreign legal opinion. This is accomplished by determining whether or not a foreign 
legal opinion is on file with [[DTC]]. 
 
Checks Performed during Foreign Legal Opinion Review 
The [[issuer]]’s country of origin or issue’s country of origin should exist in the foreign legal opinion list for approval.  
If the country in question is not on the foreign legal opinion list, [[DTC]]’s legal department must obtain the legal 
opinion and update the foreign legal opinion list. 
If it is not present in the legal opinion list, the issue cannot be approved. 
Foreign Legal Opinion Review Process 
We will now identify when foreign legal opinion review is initiated in UW SOURCE. 
Foreign legal opinion reviews occur multiple times during eligibility processing.  
Foreign Legal Opinion Review is initiated via UW SOURCE each time: 
• An issue is created 
• An issue is validated for approval 
• An issue is validated for closing 
• An issue or security are modified 
• One or more country fields are modified or enriched 
Subsequent to foreign legal opinion being available on file with [[DTC]], the [[issuer]] cannot be sent for approval 
and closing. 
Foreign Legal Opinion compliance can be determined automatically by UW SOURCE through systemic 
checks, or can be manually specified by the legal reviewer during the review process. 
For each country field that contains a valid country code, UW SOURCE systemically checks whether the 
country code is foreign legal opinion compliant.  

---

## Page 253

UW SOURCE: Eligibility Processing – Part 5 
Foreign Legal Opinion Compliance 253 
UW SOURCE checks the compliance against the Compliance database maintained in the Security Master File 
(SMF) system. 
If the systemic check fails, the legal reviewer reviews the country information and marks the issue as 
Compliant or Not Compliant as appropriate.  
UW SOURCE treats user assigned compliance as equivalent to system assigned compliance. 
If foreign legal opinion compliance checks are not successful, the issue cannot be approved or closed. 
Status of an Issue During FLO Review 
Once issues have been verified for compliancy, the compliancy status is updated in UW SOURCE. 
The status of the issue is set to one of the following: 
• Compliant By System and Not Compliant By System (applicable in the case of systemic checks)  
• Compliant By User and Not Compliant By User (applicable in the case of  checks specified by the legal 
reviewer) 
Viewing the Foreign Legal Opinion Compliance Status of an Issue 
To view the Foreign Legal Opinion Compliance status of an issue: 
1. From the Eligibility menu, choose Search Issue. 
 The Search Issue for Eligibility Review page appears. 
2. In the Search Criteria section, enter a valid [[issuer]] Type, and [[issuer]] Eligible Status or Issue [[ID]], and click 
Search. 
The Issue List section appears. 
3. Select the required issue and from the Action drop-down list, select View Foreign Legal Compliance, and 
click Go. 
The View Foreign Legal Opinion Compliance page appears. 

---

## Page 254

UW SOURCE: Eligibility Processing – Part 5 
Foreign Legal Opinion Compliance 254 
 
Figure 49: View Foreign Legal Opinion Compliance Page 
4. In the Compliance Information section, note the compliancy status of the issue. 
5. To view the offering details, click View Offering. 
6. To view the issue details, click View Issue.  
7. To return to the View Foreign Legal Opinion Compliance page, click Cancel. 
View Foreign Legal Opinion Compliance Page 
The View Foreign Legal Opinion Compliance page enables you to view the details of the compliance status at 
both the issue level, as well as at the security level. 
The sections available on this page are: 
• Issue Identification Information 
• [[issuer]] Identification Information 
• Issue Level Compliance 
• Security Level Compliance List 
• Compliance Information

• Debt Unit 
A security usually comprised of two other [[securities]] (the components) 
traded together; generally they consist of corporate bond and a 
warrant. The unit can separate into the components immediately upon 
issuance or trade on its own until such time that the [[issuer]] decides to 
separate the unit into the components. The separation can be 
voluntary or mandatory.  
• Corporate Insured Custodial Receipt 
Corporate [[Bonds]] are insured by insurance [[Companies]]. Corporate 
insured receipts represent the insured portion and are similar to the 
original corporate bond except for the security identification number. 
• Collateralized Mortgage Obligation(CMO) 
A type of mortgage backed security. 
• [[Asset]]-Backed Security ([[ABS]])  
A type of security backed by a pool of credit card receivables, home-
[[Equity]] loans, or other assets. 
• Government Security 
[[securities]] issued by any government agency.  
• Corporate Debt Derivative  
 A hybrid security whose value is based on the performance of the 
underlying instrument. Debt Derivative trades in the denominated 
currency. 

---

## Page 258

UW SOURCE: Eligibility Processing – Part 5 
Foreign Legal Opinion Compliance 258 
Field Name Field Description 
• Municipal Bond 
A debt obligation issued by municipality or local government agency. 
• Municipal VRDO 
Municipal Variable Rate Demand [[obligations]] (VRDO) is floating rate 
instruments with terms of as much as 40 years. They pay interest 
monthly or quarterly based on a floating rate that is reset daily or 
weekly based on an index of short-term municipal rates. 
• Municipal Note 
Shorter maturity period between issuance and redemption than 
amunicipal bond. Maturity period is one year or less.  
• Municipal Insured Custodial Receipt 
Municipal [[Bonds]] are insured by American Municipal Bond Assurance 
Corporation (AMBAC) or MBIA. A municipal insured custodial receipt 
represents the insured portion and is similar to the original municipal 
bond except for the security identification number. 
• Municipal Other Tax Exempt 
Municipal Other Tax Exempt is debt [[obligations]] that are issued by the 
state, county, city, or any other political sub-division. These [[Bonds]] are 
attractive to individual investors, since, as the name indicates, the 
government does not tax the interest received. 
Series [[ID]] The Series [[ID]] field has a maximum length of 10 characters. [[securities]] within 
an Issue can have different Expected Closing Dates and these [[securities]] are 
grouped under a level called Series.

This section displays all the information regarding compliance of an issue as well as the compliance check

Field Name Field Description 
Action Date The date on which action was performed. 
Compliance Check Status Valid values: 
• Compliant By System 
The compliance check is done by UW SOURCE and the issue is

• Not Compliant By System 
Indicates that the compliance check is done by UW SOURCE and the

• Compliant By User

issue is not compliant.

reference. 
Internal Comments The comments entered by the [[DTC]] staff for [[DTC]]’s own reference. 
User [[ID]] The [[DTC]] Staff login details. 
Reviewing Issues for Foreign Legal Opinion Compliance  
When the systemic compliance check has failed, the [[DTC]] legal user can choose to review the foreign legal 
opinion compliance of the issue and approve it.  
Based on the review, the [[DTC]] legal reviewer can update the compliancy status to Compliant By User or Not 
Compliant By User as applicable. 
To review issues for foreign legal opinion compliance: 
1. From the Eligibility menu, choose Search Issue. 
 The Search Issue for Eligibility Review page appears. 
2. In the Search Criteria section, enter a valid [[issuer]] Type and [[issuer]] Eligible Status, or Issue [[ID]]. 
3. Click Expand Search and set the Foreign Legal Opinion Compliance status to Not Compliant by System. 
4. Click Search. 
 The Issue List section appears. 
5. Select a required issue and from the Action drop-down list, select Review Foreign Legal Compliance, and 
click Go. 

---

## Page 261

UW SOURCE: Eligibility Processing – Part 5 
Foreign Legal Opinion Compliance 261 
 The Review Foreign Legal Opinion Compliance page appears. 
 
Figure 50: Review Foreign Legal Opinion Compliance Page 
6. Select the appropriate compliancy status, and enter the Internal and External Comments and click Submit. 
7. To view the offering details, click View Offering. 
8. To view issue details, click View Issue.  
The Review Foreign Legal Opinion Compliance Page 
The Review Foreign Legal Opinion Compliance page enables you to review the Foreign Legal Compliance of 
the issue and approve it. This process is invoked when the systematic compliance check fails. 
The sections available on this page are: 
Issue Identification Section 
Table 167: View Foreign Legal Opinion Compliance – Issue Identification section 
Field Name Field Description 
Issue [[ID]] The unique identifier assigned to the issue by UW SOURCE. 
Sub Issue Type The type of Security. 
Valid values: 
• Common [[Stock]] 
A security that represents ownership in a corporation. Owners of 
Common [[Stock]] are on the bottom of the priority ladder of the ownership 


---

## Page 262

UW SOURCE: Eligibility Processing – Part 5 
Foreign Legal Opinion Compliance 262 
Field Name Field Description 
structure. 
• Preferred  [[Stock]] 
A class of ownership in a corporation with a stated [[Dividend]] that must 
be paid before dividends to owners of Common [[Stock]]. Preferred [[Stock]] 
owners usually [[DO]] not have [[voting rights]]. Preferred [[Stock]] holders carry 
additional rights above and beyond those conferred on a Common 
[[Stock]]. 
• Limited Partnership 
A limited partnership consists of one or more general partners, who 
manage the business and assume legal debts and [[obligations]], and 
one or more limited partners who are liable only to the extent of their 
investments. These investors are not liable for legal action or debts 
the company may face. 
• American Depositary Receipt ([[ADR]]) 
A negotiable security issued by a US commercial bank that represents 
a specified number of [[shares]] in a foreign [[Stock]] that is traded in a US 
exchange. ADRs are denominated in US dollars. 
• [[Equity]] [[Derivatives]] 
 A hybrid security whose value is based on the performance of the 
underlying instrument. [[Equity]] Derivative trades in [[shares]]/ units. 
• Closed End Fund 
A type of fund having a limited number of [[shares]] listed on a [[Stock]] 
exchange. Closed funds redeem [[shares]] on a continuous basis. 
• Institutional Money Market Instrument (IMMI) 
A mutual fund whose investments are in high-yield money market 
instrumentssuch as federal [[securities]], CDs and commercial paper. Its 
intent is tomake such instruments, normally purchased in large 
denominations by [[Institutions]],available indirectly to individuals.  
• [[Equity]] Unit 
A security usually comprised of two other [[securities]] (the components) 
traded together; generally they consist of common [[Stock]] and a warrant. 
The unit can separate into the components immediately upon issuance 
or trade on its own until such time that the [[issuer]] decides to separate 
the unit into the components. The separation can be voluntary or 
mandatory. 
• Unit Investment Trust 

---

## Page 263

UW SOURCE: Eligibility Processing – Part 5 
Foreign Legal Opinion Compliance 263 
Field Name Field Description 
An investment company that offers a fixed, unmanaged portfolio, 
generally of stocks and [[Bonds]], as redeemable "units" to investors for 
a specific period of time. It is designed to provide capital appreciation 
and/or [[Dividend]] income. 
• Exchange Traded Funds 
A security that tracks an index, a commodity or a basket of assets like 
an index fund, but trades like a [[Stock]] on an exchange. ETFs 
experience price changes throughout the day as they are bought and 
sold. 
• [[Warrants]] 
A warrant is a security that entitles its owner to buy a share of the 
issuing company's [[Stock]] at a predetermined price regardless of the 
[[Stock]]’s current market value.  [[Warrants]] are issued to enhance the 
future value of the [[Stock]] to the holder. 
• Depository [[shares]] 
A depository share represents ownership in the [[shares]] of a non-U.S. 
company that trades in U.S. financial markets. 
Like [[ADR]]'s Depository [[shares]] are created when [[shares]] of U.S. 
corporation are placed into a trust account or with a custodian bank.  
The [[securities]] issued are termed to be Depository [[shares]]. 
• Rights 
A security giving stockholders [[Entitlement]] to purchase new [[shares]] 
issued by [[The Corporation]] at a predetermined price (normally less than 
the current market price) in proportion to the number of [[shares]] 
already owned. Rights are issued only for a short period of time, after 
which they expire. 
• Corporate Bond 
A debt obligation or IOU issued by public or private corporation that 
obligates the [[issuer]] to pay the bondholder principal plus interest. 
• Corporate VRDO  
Corporate Variable Rate Demand [[obligations]] (VRDO) is floating rate 
instrument. They pay interest monthly or quarterly based on a floating 
rate that is reset daily or weekly based on an index of short-term 
corporate debt rates.

of [[shares]] in a foreign [[Stock]] that is traded in a US exchange. ADRs are denominated in US 
dollars. 
• [[Equity]] [[Derivatives]] 
 A hybrid security whose value is based on the performance of the underlying instrument. 
[[Equity]] Derivative trades in [[shares]]/ units. 
• Closed End Fund 
A type of fund having a limited number of [[shares]] listed on a [[Stock Exchange]]. Closed funds 
redeem [[shares]] on a continuous basis. 
• Institutional Money Market Instrument (IMMI) 
A mutual fund whose investments are in high-yield money market instrumentssuch as 
federal [[securities]], CDs and commercial paper. Its intent is tomake such instruments, 
normally purchased in large denominations by [[Institutions]],available indirectly to individuals.  
• [[Equity]] Unit 
A security usually comprised of two other [[securities]] (the components) traded together; 
generally they consist of common [[Stock]] and a warrant. The unit can separate into the 
components immediately upon issuance or trade on its own until such time that the [[issuer]] 
decides to separate the unit into the components. The separation can be voluntary or 
mandatory. 
• Unit Investment Trust 
An investment company that offers a fixed, unmanaged portfolio, generally of stocks and 
[[Bonds]], as redeemable "units" to investors for a specific period of time. It is designed to 
provide capital appreciation and/or [[Dividend]] income. 
• Exchange Traded Funds

A security that tracks an index, a commodity or a basket of assets like an index fund, but

A warrant is a security that entitles its owner to buy a share of the issuing company's [[Stock]] 
at a predetermined price regardless of the [[Stock]]’s current market value.  [[Warrants]] are 
issued to enhance the future value of the [[Stock]] to the holder. 
• Depository [[shares]] 
A depository share represents ownership in the [[shares]] of a non-U.S. company that trades 
in U.S. financial markets. 
Like [[ADR]]'s Depository [[shares]] are created when [[shares]] of U.S. corporation are placed into 
a trust account or with a custodian bank.  The [[securities]] issued are termed to be 
Depository [[shares]]. 
• Rights 
A security giving stockholders [[Entitlement]] to purchase new [[shares]] issued by the 
corporation at a predetermined price (normally less than the current market price) in 
proportion to the number of [[shares]] already owned. Rights are issued only for a short period 
of time, after which they expire. 
• Corporate Bond 
A debt obligation or IOU issued by public or private corporation that obligates the [[issuer]] to 
pay the bondholder principal plus interest. 
• Corporate VRDO  
Corporate Variable Rate Demand [[obligations]] (VRDO) is floating rate instrument. They pay 
interest monthly or quarterly based on a floating rate that is reset daily or weekly based on 
an index of short-term corporate debt rates. 
• Certificates of Deposit (CD) 
CDs are time deposit products that are FDIC insured and have flexible maturity periods 
ranging from seven days to 14 years. 
• Debt Unit 
A security usually comprised of two other [[securities]] (the components) traded together; 
generally they consist of corporate bond and a warrant. The unit can separate into the 
components immediately upon issuance or trade on its own until such time that the [[issuer]]

## Page 281

UW SOURCE: Eligibility Processing – Part 5 
Office Of Foreign [[Asset]] Control (OFAC) Compliance 281 
10. OFFICE OF FOREIGN [[Asset]] CONTROL (OFAC) 
COMPLIANCE 
The Office of Foreign Assets Control (OFAC) of the U.S. Department of Treasury administers and enforces a 
series of laws that impose economic sanctions designed to further U.S. foreign policy and national security 
objectives. All issues and [[securities]] at [[DTC]] are subject to an OFAC compliance review as part of eligibility 
processing. 
Sanctions are imposed against targeted foreign countries and territories, as well as specially designated 
nationals (SDNs) and blocked persons, which may include terrorists, international narcotics traffickers, and those 
engaged in activities related to the proliferation of weapons of mass destruction.   
All financial [[Institutions]], including US banks and subsidiaries of US banks, are required to comply with the 
economic sanctions and embargo programs administered under regulations issued by OFAC. 
OFAC Regulations 
OFAC regulations require the blocking of accounts and other assets of specified countries, entities, and 
individuals. They also prohibit any unlicensed trade and financial transactions with specified countries, entities, 
and individuals. 
OFAC Compliance at [[DTC]] 
[[DTC]] is prohibited from doing business with any individuals appearing on the OFAC list.  
As part of eligibility processing at [[DTC]], UW SOURCE automatically verifies the OFAC compliancy of an issue. 
During the OFAC compliance process, the OFAC lists will be compared when an AML-sensitive data element 
such as [[issuer]] country of origin is made available. 
[[DTC]]’s Office of Corporate and Regulatory Compliance (OCRC) is responsible for overseeing [[DTC]]’s OFAC 
compliance program.   
The operations departments within [[DTC]] are responsible for day-to-day OFAC monitoring. This entails 
monitoring accounts or transactions in order to detect any OFAC-banned activity, and report any suspected 
violations to the OCRC.   
The functions of OCRC under the OFAC Compliance program are to : 
• Identify assets and transactions that are required to be blocked. For this, OCRC utilizes a software product 
of Accuity Solutions, the mainframe FACFilter. 
• Investigate exceptions reported by the various operations departments and determine the appropriate follow-
up action(s). 
  

---

## Page 282

UW SOURCE: Eligibility Processing – Part 5 
Office Of Foreign [[Asset]] Control (OFAC) Compliance 282 
OFAC Compliance Process in UW SOURCE 
You will now learn how OFAC compliance processing is performed in UW SOURCE. 
All new issues are automatically scanned for possible OFAC violations at regularly scheduled intervals.  
The issue and security information that requires an OFAC scan is sent to the [[DTC]]-OFAC system as soon as it 
is available or enriched in the UW SOURCE. 
During this process, new issue data is extracted on a daily basis at intervals of 15 minutes starting at 8 a.m. 
and ending at 6 p.m.  Each time an issue is added via UW SOURCE, it is included in the next scheduled 
extraction.  If the information extracted is subsequently modified, the issue will not be rescanned. 
Immediately after extraction, each resulting file is scanned by the TFP FACFilter software for possible OFAC 
violations.  
The resulting OFAC scan report is stored in [[DTC]]-OFAC system and is directly accessible through UW 
SOURCE. 
If information is not OFAC complaint, a work item is generated for Eligibility Supervisor to review it and resolve 
the OFAC check failures in UW SOURCE. 
Stages in OFAC Compliance Processing  
All issues that are determined to be non-compliant during the initial scan are subject to additional 
examinations. 
Altogether, there are four possible stages to OFAC compliance processing in UW SOURCE.  They are: 
• Online OFAC Scan Approval 
• Detailed OFAC Batch Scan 
• Review OFAC Scan Details 
• OFAC Audit Details 
Table 182: Stages in OFAC Compliance processing 
Stage Description 
Online OFAC 
Scan Approval   
The first step in checking for OFAC compliance is checking the issue and security level 
information to eliminate all issues that are compliant so that the eligibility process can 
continue; if there is a potential hit then a detailed scan is done. 
Detailed OFAC 
Batch Scan  
For the issues marked for detailed OFAC scan, the [[DTC]]-OFAC system reads the items at 
regular intervals and performs a detailed scan for OFAC violations.  
The results of the detailed scan are stored in the [[DTC]]-OFAC system which then provides 
information on the OFAC compliancy of the issue to UW SOURCE. 
Review OFAC 
Scan Details 
Issues that are identified as not being OFAC complaint in the detailed OFAC Batch Scan 
are reviewed by the Eligibility Specialist.  
The Eligibility Specialist will view the scan details and determine if the scan details are valid 
or not, and whether it can be resolved. The issue will not be eligible till the time potential or 
realized OFAC violations are resolved. 

---

## Page 283

UW SOURCE: Eligibility Processing – Part 5 
Office Of Foreign [[Asset]] Control (OFAC) Compliance 283 
Stage Description 
OFAC Audit 
Details  
 
An audit will be maintained for all the OFAC Scans done with respect to an issue. This 
audit also contains details about the resolution such as when it was done and by whom.  
At any point of time the audit details contain the complete history of the OFAC Scan results 
corresponding to an issue. 
Issue Statuses during OFAC Compliance 
An issue is assigned one of the seven possible statuses during OFAC compliance processing. The OFAC 
Status Indicator in UW SOURCE conveys the status of the issue at any point in the process. 
Table 183: Issue Status during OFAC Compliance 
Status   Description 
Pending Online 
Scan 
Indicates that the information has been sent for Online OFAC scan and is awaiting scan 
results. 
Pending 
Detailed Scan 
Indicates that the Online OFAC scan has identified a Potential Hit and so the information 
is pending for Batch scan.  
No Hit Indicates that the information has been scanned and there are no matching Stop 
Descriptor(s). 
Overridden Indicates that a new version of the information is available and the information which was 
scanned earlier or which is undergoing the scan process is no longer valid.  
In this case, irrespective of the scan results, the status of the old information for which 
scan result or review is pending will be set to Overridden. 
Potential Hit Indicates that the information has been scanned and flagged with one or more matching 
Stop Descriptor(s). 
False Positive Indicates that the information was flagged and resolved by the Eligibility Specialist as 
cleared. 
True Hit 
 
Indicates that the information was flagged as an OFAC violation and resolved by the 
Eligibility Specialist. 
Not Applicable Indicates that OFAC is not applicable for the Issue 
Viewing the OFAC Compliance Status of an Issue 
To view the OFAC compliance of an issue: 
1. From the Eligibility menu, choose Search Issue. 
The Search Issue for Eligibility Review page appears. 
2. In the Search Criteria  section, enter a valid [[issuer]] Type  and [[issuer]] Eligible Status or Issue [[ID]] and click 
Search. 
3. In the Issue List section, select the required issue and from the Action drop-down list, select View OFAC 
Compliance and click Go.

Reviewing the Office Of Foreign [[Asset]] Control (OFAC) Compliance of 
an Issue 
In UW SOURCE, potential hits must be reviewed by the Eligibility Specialist and eventually set to a final status 
of either False Positive (FP) or True Hit (TH).  
The Eligibility Specialist can view the scan details and determine if the scan details are valid or not and 
whether the situation can be resolved. 
The issue can be eligible for approval once the probable or realized OFAC violations are resolved. 
After reviewing the ‘hit’ the Eligibility Specialist updates the OFAC Status Indicator to FP, TH, or PR, as 
applicable. 

---

## Page 290

UW SOURCE: Eligibility Processing – Part 5 
Office Of Foreign [[Asset]] Control (OFAC) Compliance 290 
If the OFAC Status Indicator for a given issue has been set to FP, the [[Underwriting]] staff can then make the 
issue eligible for [[DTC]] services.   
If the OFAC Status Indicator for the issue is NH, OFAC review is not necessary and the issue can be made 
eligible.  
To resolve the true hit, the Eligibility Specialist must notify his/her supervisor.  The supervisor will review the 
Stop descriptor in OFAC and determine if the OFAC Status Indicator should be updated to FP or TH. 
The supervisor will then make the update accordingly. If the supervisor determines that it is a TH, OCRC will 
be notified via e-mail.  
To review the OFAC compliance of an issue: 
1. From the Eligibility menu, choose Search Issue. 
The Search Issue for Eligibility Review page appears. 
2. In the Search  Criteria section, enter a valid [[issuer]] Type, [[issuer]] Eligible Status and set the OFAC 
Compliance status to Potential Hit False Positive, True Hit, or Issue [[ID]]. 
3. In the Issue List section, select the required issue and from the Action drop-down list, select Review OFAC 
Compliance and click Go. 
The Review OFAC Compliance page appears. 
 
Figure 54: Review OFAC Compliance Page 
Note. The page displays the OFAC Compliance details in review mode.

[[issuer]] decides to separate the unit into the components. The separation can be 
voluntary or mandatory. 
• Unit Investment Trust 
An investment company that offers a fixed, unmanaged portfolio, generally of stocks 
and [[Bonds]], as redeemable "units" to investors for a specific period of time. It is 
designed to provide capital appreciation and/or [[Dividend]] income. 
• Exchange Traded Funds 
A security that tracks an index, a commodity or a basket of assets like an index fund, 
but trades like a [[Stock]] on an exchange. ETFs experience price changes throughout 
the day as they are bought and sold. 
• [[Warrants]] 
A warrant is a security that entitles its owner to buy a share of the issuing company's 
[[Stock]] at a predetermined price regardless of the [[Stock]]’s current market value.  
[[Warrants]] are issued to enhance the future value of the [[Stock]] to the holder. 
• Depository [[shares]] 
A depository share represents ownership in the [[shares]] of a non-U.S. company that 
trades in U.S. financial markets. 
Like [[ADR]]'s Depository [[shares]] are created when [[shares]] of U.S. corporation are 
placed into a trust account or with a custodian bank.  The [[securities]] issued are termed 
to be Depository [[shares]]. 
• Rights 
A security giving stockholders [[Entitlement]] to purchase new [[shares]] issued by the 
corporation at a predetermined price (normally less than the current market price) in 

---

## Page 293

UW SOURCE: Eligibility Processing – Part 5 
Office Of Foreign [[Asset]] Control (OFAC) Compliance 293 
Field Name Field Description 
proportion to the number of [[shares]] already owned. Rights are issued only for a short 
period of time, after which they expire. 
• Corporate Bond 
A debt obligation or IOU issued by public or private corporation that obligates the 
[[issuer]] to pay the bondholder principal plus interest. 
• Corporate VRDO  
Corporate Variable Rate Demand [[obligations]] (VRDO) is floating rate instrument. 
They pay interest monthly or quarterly based on a floating rate that is reset daily or 
weekly based on an index of short-term corporate debt rates. 
• Certificates of Deposit (CD) 
CDs are time deposit products that are FDIC insured and have flexible maturity 
periods ranging from seven days to 14 years. 
• Debt Unit 
A security usually comprised of two other [[securities]] (the components) traded 
together; generally they consist of corporate bond and a warrant. The unit can 
separate into the components immediately upon issuance or trade on its own until 
such time that the [[issuer]] decides to separate the unit into the components. The 
separation can be voluntary or mandatory.  
• Corporate Insured Custodial Receipt 
Corporate [[Bonds]] are insured by insurance [[Companies]]. Corporate insured receipts 
represent the insured portion and are similar to the original corporate bond except for 
the security identification number. 
• Collateralized Mortgage Obligation(CMO) 
A type of mortgage backed security. 
• [[Asset]]-Backed Security ([[ABS]])  
A type of security backed by a pool of credit card receivables, home-[[Equity]] loans, or 
other assets. 
• Government Security 
[[securities]] issued by any government agency.  
• Corporate Debt Derivative  
 A hybrid security whose value is based on the performance of the underlying 
instrument. Debt Derivative trades in the denominated currency. 
• Municipal Bond

Field Name Field Description 
A debt obligation issued by municipality or local government agency. 
• Municipal VRDO 
Municipal Variable Rate Demand [[obligations]] (VRDO) is floating rate instruments with 
terms of as much as 40 years. They pay interest monthly or quarterly based on a 
floating rate that is reset daily or weekly based on an index of short-term municipal 
rates.

Shorter maturity period between issuance and redemption than amunicipal bond.

identification number. 
• Municipal Other Tax Exempt 
Municipal Other Tax Exempt is debt [[obligations]] tha t are issued by the state, county, 
city, or any other political sub- division. These [[Bonds]] are attractive to individual 
investors, since, as the name indicates, the government does not tax the interest 
received.

## Page 308

UW SOURCE: Eligibility Processing – Part 5 
Legal Review of Ownership Restricted Issues 308 
11. LEGAL REVIEW OF OWNERSHIP RESTRICTED 
ISSUES 
Concepts 
Certain corporate [[Equity]] issues belong to a group of industries on which legal restrictions are imposed on the 
quantity of [[Stock]] that can be owned by a class of investors. Such issues should be subject to a legal review and 
approved before the issue is approved. 
This is a significant review process because if ownership restrictions are not reviewed and approved, issues 
cannot be approved and closed.  
The issues to which ownership restrictions are applicable are: 
• Communication 
• Maritime 
• Aviation 
• Gaming 
• Real Estate Investment Trusts (REITs) 
• Others 
Monitoring Mechanisms for Ownership Restrictions 
At the time of creating or modifying a deal, underwriters must declare whether ownership restrictions are 
applicable for the issue. This can be done via the Issue Details page in UW SOURCE.  
For issues to which ownership restrictions apply, underwriters must submit a legal review response. The types 
of legal review response to ownership restrictions are: 
• Dual certificates 
• Segregation Account 100 (SEG 100) 
• Employee [[Stock]] Ownership Plan (ESOP) 
• Reject 
During legal review, the legal user specifies SEG 100/Dual certificate legal response for certain ownership 
restrictions only. This is applicable to aviation, communication and maritime and is not applicable to gaming and 
REIT. 
  

---

## Page 309

UW SOURCE: Eligibility Processing – Part 5 
Legal Review of Ownership Restricted Issues 309 
Table 196: Legal Review Responses 
Legal Review Response Description 
Dual Certificate Dual certificate is applicable when separate certificates are printed for 
domestic and alien investors. Dual certificates can used to monitor 
ownership restrictions. 
Segregation Account 100 (SEG 
100) 
SEG 100 is applicable when the [[securities]] are segregated into a 
separate account. These [[securities]] are not eligible for [[DTC]] services 
until they are released. 
Employee [[Stock]] Ownership Plan 
(ESOP) 
ESOP is applicable only for REITs. 
Reject Reject is applicable when the legal review determines that the security 
is not monitored through any of the above mechanisms and does not 
meet the eligibility criteria for ownership restricted issues.  
 
Table 197: Ownership Restriction 
S. No. Ownership Restriction SEG100/Dual Certificate Legal Response Applicability 
1.  Aviation Yes 
2.  Communication Yes 
3.  Maritime Yes 
4.  Gaming No 
5.  REIT No 
 
Conducting Legal Review of Ownership Restrictions 
The process for legal review of Ownership Restrictions is as follows: 
1. Underwriters should declare ownership restrictions that are applicable for the issue as a part of creating or 
enriching deal information. The Eligibility Specialist reviews the offering documentation and determines if 
ownership restrictions are applicable for the issue.  
2. UW SOURCE generates work items for the legal department.  
3. As part of the legal review, [[DTC]] determines how the ownership restrictions will be monitored. The [[DTC]] legal 
user may choose to approve, reject or return the issue. 
4. If the monitoring mechanisms are deemed satisfactory to comply with the legal restrictions, the status of the 
issue is updated to Approved. The [[DTC]] legal user may attach an ownership restrictions letter during 
approval. 
5. Legal review may result in rejection of ownership restricted issues if they [[DO]] not meet the required monitoring 
guidelines. An ownership restricted issue that is rejected in the legal review process cannot be approved by 
the Eligibility Specialist. 
6. The legal user must enter internal comments for Reject and Return. 

---

## Page 310

UW SOURCE: Eligibility Processing – Part 5 
Legal Review of Ownership Restricted Issues 310 
7. A work-item is created in UW SOURCE for the Eligibility Specialist to reject or return the issue (Work-item - 
Ownership Restrictions Rejected/Returned), and a notification is sent to the [[underwriter]]. 
Issue Statuses During Legal Review of Ownership Restrictions 
 
During the legal review of ownership restrictions, the issue can be in any one of the following statuses: 
• Review Not Required 
• Pending For Review 
• Approved 
• Rejected 
• Returned 
Table 198: Issue Status during Legal Review of Ownership Restriction 
Status Description 
Review Not Required Ownership restrictions [[DO]] not apply to the issue. Hence, no legal review is 
required. 
Pending For Review  Ownership restrictions [[DO]] not apply to the issue. It is pending for review. 
Approved Ownership restrictions pertaining to the issue was reviewed and approved. 
Rejected Ownership restrictions pertaining to the issue was reviewed and rejected. 
Returned Ownership restrictions pertaining to the issue was reviewed and returned as 
Not an Ownership Restricted Issue. The Ownership Restrictions field must 
be set to None.

## Page 312

UW SOURCE: Eligibility Processing – Part 5 
Legal Review of Ownership Restricted Issues 312 
The View Ownership Restrictions page enables you to view the details of the ownership restrictions of an issue. 
The sections available on this page are: 
• Issue Identification Information 
• Offering Information 
• [[issuer]] Information 
• Ownership Restriction Information 
• Response Information 
Issue Identification Information Section 
This section displays the issue identification information. 
Table 199: View Ownership Restriction - Issue Identification Information Section 
Field Name Field Description 
Issue [[ID]] The unique identifier assigned to the issue by UW SOURCE 
Ownership 
Restriction Review 
Status 
Valid values: 
• Review Not Required 
Indicates that the issue does have any ownership restrictions and 
does not require a legal review. 
• Pending For Review 
Indicates that the issue has ownership restrictions and is pending for 
review. 
• Approved 
Indicates that the ownership restrictions of the issue was reviewed and 
approved. 
• Rejected 
Indicates that the ownership restrictions of the issue was reviewed and 
rejected. 
• Returned 
Indicates that the ownership restrictions of the issue was reviewed and 
returned as Not An Ownership Restricted Issue. 
Sub Issue Type The type of Security. 
Valid values: 
• Common [[Stock]] 
A security that represents ownership in a corporation. Owners of Common 
[[Stock]] are on the bottom of the priority ladder of the ownership structure. 
• Preferred  [[Stock]] 
A class of ownership in a corporation with a stated [[Dividend]] that must be paid 
before dividends to owners of Common [[Stock]]. Preferred [[Stock]] owners usually 
[[DO]] not have [[voting rights]]. Preferred [[Stock]] holders carry additional rights above 
and beyond those conferred on a Common [[Stock]].

## Page 313

UW SOURCE: Eligibility Processing – Part 5 
Legal Review of Ownership Restricted Issues 313 
Field Name Field Description 
• Limited Partnership 
A limited partnership consists of one or more general partners, who manage 
the business and assume legal debts and [[obligations]], and one or more 
limited partners who are liable only to the extent of their investments. These 
investors are not liable for legal action or debts the company may face. 
• American Depositary Receipt ([[ADR]]) 
A negotiable security issued by a US commercial bank that represents a 
specified number of [[shares]] in a foreign [[Stock]] that is traded in a US 
exchange. ADRs are denominated in US dollars. 
• [[Equity]] [[Derivatives]] 
 A hybrid security whose value is based on the performance of the underlying 
instrument. [[Equity]] Derivative trades in [[shares]]/ units. 
• Closed End Fund 
A type of fund having a limited number of [[shares]] listed on a [[Stock Exchange]]. 
Closed funds redeem [[shares]] on a continuous basis. 
• Institutional Money Market Instrument (IMMI) 
A mutual fund whose investments are in high-yield money market 
instrumentssuch as federal [[securities]], CDs and commercial paper. Its intent 
is tomake such instruments, normally purchased in large denominations by 
[[Institutions]],available indirectly to individuals.  
• [[Equity]] Unit 
A security usually comprised of two other [[securities]] (the components) traded 
together; generally they consist of common [[Stock]] and a warrant. The unit can 
separate into the components immediately upon issuance or trade on its own 
until such time that the [[issuer]] decides to separate the unit into the 
components. The separation can be voluntary or mandatory. 
• Unit Investment Trust 
An investment company that offers a fixed, unmanaged portfolio, generally of 
stocks and [[Bonds]], as redeemable "units" to investors for a specific period of 
time. It is designed to provide capital appreciation and/or [[Dividend]] income. 
• Exchange Traded Funds 
A security that tracks an index, a commodity or a basket of assets like an 
index fund, but trades like a [[Stock]] on an exchange. ETFs experience price 
changes throughout the day as they are bought and sold. 
• [[Warrants]] 

---

## Page 314

UW SOURCE: Eligibility Processing – Part 5 
Legal Review of Ownership Restricted Issues 314 
Field Name Field Description 
A warrant is a security that entitles its owner to buy a share of the issuing 
company's [[Stock]] at a predetermined price regardless of the [[Stock]]’s current 
market value.  [[Warrants]] are issued to enhance the future value of the [[Stock]] 
to the holder. 
• Depository [[shares]] 
A depository share represents ownership in the [[shares]] of a non-U.S. 
company that trades in U.S. financial markets. 
Like [[ADR]]'s Depository [[shares]] are created when [[shares]] of U.S. corporation 
are placed into a trust account or with a custodian bank.  The [[securities]] 
issued are termed to be Depository [[shares]]. 
• Rights 
A security giving stockholders [[Entitlement]] to purchase new [[shares]] issued by 
[[The Corporation]] at a predetermined price (normally less than the current 
market price) in proportion to the number of [[shares]] already owned. Rights 
are issued only for a short period of time, after which they expire. 
• Corporate Bond 
A debt obligation or IOU issued by public or private corporation that obligates 
the [[issuer]] to pay the bondholder principal plus interest. 
• Corporate VRDO  
Corporate Variable Rate Demand [[obligations]] (VRDO) is floating rate 
instrument. They pay interest monthly or quarterly based on a floating rate 
that is reset daily or weekly based on an index of short-term corporate debt 
rates. 
• Certificates of Deposit (CD) 
CDs are time deposit products that are FDIC insured and have flexible 
maturity periods ranging from seven days to 14 years. 
• Debt Unit 
A security usually comprised of two other [[securities]] (the components) traded 
together; generally they consist of corporate bond and a warrant. The unit 
can separate into the components immediately upon issuance or trade on its 
own until such time that the [[issuer]] decides to separate the unit into the 
components. The separation can be voluntary or mandatory.  
• Corporate Insured Custodial Receipt 

---

## Page 315

UW SOURCE: Eligibility Processing – Part 5 
Legal Review of Ownership Restricted Issues 315 
Field Name Field Description 
Corporate [[Bonds]] are insured by insurance [[Companies]]. Corporate insured 
receipts represent the insured portion and are similar to the original 
corporate bond except for the security identification number. 
• Collateralized Mortgage Obligation(CMO) 
A type of mortgage backed security. 
• [[Asset]]-Backed Security ([[ABS]])  
A type of security backed by a pool of credit card receivables, home-[[Equity]] 
loans, or other assets. 
• Government Security 
[[securities]] issued by any government agency.  
• Corporate Debt Derivative  
 A hybrid security whose value is based on the performance of the underlying 
instrument. Debt Derivative trades in the denominated currency. 
• Municipal Bond 
A debt obligation issued by municipality or local government agency. 
• Municipal VRDO 
Municipal Variable Rate Demand [[obligations]] (VRDO) is floating rate 
instruments with terms of as much as 40 years. They pay interest monthly or 
quarterly based on a floating rate that is reset daily or weekly based on an 
index of short-term municipal rates. 
• Municipal Note 
Shorter maturity period between issuance and redemption than amunicipal 
bond. Maturity period is one year or less.  
• Municipal Insured Custodial Receipt 
Municipal [[Bonds]] are insured by American Municipal Bond Assurance 
Corporation (AMBAC) or MBIA. A municipal insured custodial receipt 
represents the insured portion and is similar to the original municipal bond 
except for the security identification number. 
• Municipal Other Tax Exempt 
Municipal Other Tax Exempt is debt [[obligations]] that are issued by the state, 
county, city, or any other political sub -division. These [[Bonds]] are attractive to 
individual investors, since, as the name indicates, the government does not 
tax the interest received.

Field Name Field Description 
Document 
Date

Offering 
Document 
Type 
The type of document to be sent to make the [[securities]] in the offering eligible at [[DTC]]. 
Valid values: 
• Certificate  
A [[Stock]] certificate (also known as certificate of [[Stock]] or share certificate) is a 
legal document that certifies ownership of a specific number of [[Stock]] [[shares]] 
(or fractions thereof) in a corporation. 
• Indenture 
A formal agreement between the [[issuer]] of [[Bonds]] and the bondholders about 
the terms of the debt. 
• Offering Memorandum 
A legal document stating the objectives, risks and terms of investment 
involved with a [[Private Placement]]. This includes items such as the financial 
statements, management biographies, detailed description of the business, 
etc. An offering memorandum serves to provide buyers with information on 
the offering and to protect the sellers from the liability associated with selling 
unregistered [[securities]]. 
• Official Statement 
A shortened registration statement required by the [[securities]] and Exchange 
Commission on municipal  debt issues  
• Ordinance 
A law made by a non-sovereign body such as a city council or a colony. 
• Placement Agreement 
The sale of [[securities]] directly to private persons, and/or institutional investors, 
outside a [[public offering]]. Unlike a [[public offering]], a [[Private Placement]] does 
not have to be registered with the [[SEC]]. 
• [[Private Placement]] Memorandum 
[[Private Placement]] Memorandum is the document that describes the sale of 
an entire issue of [[securities]] to a small group of investors. 
• Prospectus 
A document that provides details about a new offering of [[securities]] for sale to 
the public. It gives a detailed financial background of the issuing company, 
how the proceeds of the [[securities]] will be used, and other pertinent 
information investors will need to make an informed decision. 
• Series Declaration

Reviewing Ownership Restrictions 
This process reviews and approves ownership restricted issues for meeting eligibility criteria for Issue Approval 
and Issue Closing. 
To review ownership restricted issues: 
1. From the Eligibility menu, choose Search Issue. 
The Search Issue for Eligibility Review page appears. 
2. In the Search Criteria section, enter a valid [[issuer]] Type and [[issuer]] Eligible Status or Issue [[ID]]. 

---

## Page 320

UW SOURCE: Eligibility Processing – Part 5 
Legal Review of Ownership Restricted Issues 320 
3. To select an appropriate Ownership Restrictions Review Status, click Expand Search. 
4. Click Search. 
5. In the Issue List section, select the required issue and from the Action drop -down list, select Review 
Ownership Restrictions and click Go. 
The Review Ownership Restrictions page appears. 
 
Figure 58: Review Ownership Restrictions Page 
6. The following actions are provided for the legal user during review: 
• Approve – The Legal User approves the Ownership Restriction and attaches an ownership restriction 
letter. 
• Reject – Eligibility Specialist as well as the Legal User rejects the ownership restrictions during the issue 
approval process. 
• Return - The Legal user finds the issue is not ownership restricted and returns the issue back to Eligibility 
Specialist.
