## Page 329

UW SOURCE: Eligibility Processing – Part 5 
Glossary 329 
GLOSSARY 
BLOR 
Blanket Letter of Representations - An agreement accepted by [[DTC]] from municipal and corporate issuers in which the 
[[issuer]] agrees to comply with the requirements stated in the Operational Arrangements, as they may be amended from time 
to time. [[DTC]] requires an [[issuer]] to submit a BLOR only once for all its future municipal or corporate issues distributed 
through [[DTC]]. 
Clearing Firm  
An organization that provides specialized comparison, clearance, and [[settlement]] services for its customers. 
Correspondent [[underwriter]] 
An [[underwriter]] that utilizes the services of a clearing firm to settle the transaction through [[DTC]]. The correspondent 
[[underwriter]] is not a [[Member]] of [[DTC]]. 
Deal  
A deal refers to the combination of an offering, its related issues, individual [[securities]], and its associated underwriters.  It is 
the basis for all of [[DTC]]’s [[Underwriting]] processes. 
Depository Trust &  Clearing Corporation  
[[DTC]] is a subsidiary of The Depository Trust & Clearing Corporation ([[DTCC]]).  It handles the [[settlement]] of trades in 
corporate and municipal [[securities]] provides [[custody]] and [[Asset]] servicing for more than two million [[securities]] issues from the 
United States and 100 other countries and territories. 
Dissemination [[underwriter]] 
The Lead or managing [[underwriter]] with responsibility to submit new issue information for dissemination. The dissemination 
agent will generally be the Lead Manager; however, in cases where there are joint lead managers, the managers must 
decide which firm will submit to [[DTC]] for regulatory purposes. 
[[FAST]] Automated [[securities]] Transfer ([[FAST]]) 
An arrangement between [[DTC]] and transfer agents to eliminate the movement of [[securities]] certificates. By signing the 
Balance Certificate Agreement with [[DTC]], agents agree to maintain [[DTC]]-eligible inventory in the form of jumbo certificates 
registered in the name of [[DTC]]'s [[nominee]] name, [[Cede & Co]]. [[DTC]] and [[FAST]] agents electronically reconcile the results of 
[[participant]]’s daily deposit and withdrawal activities. 
Final Offering Document 
The final version of an offering document issued for corporations or municipalities. It contains the complete and final 
information about the offering. 
Financial Action Task Force (FATF) 
An inter-governmental body whose purpose is the development and promotion of national and international policies to 
combat money laundering and terrorist financing. The FATF is therefore a "policy-making body," created in 1989, that 
works to generate the necessary political will to bring about legislative and regulatory reforms in these areas.  
Issue 
Stocks or [[Bonds]] sold by a corporation, municipality, or government entity. 
  

---

## Page 330

UW SOURCE: Eligibility Processing – Part 5 
Glossary 330 
[[issuer]]  
A legal entity that develops and sells [[securities]] to finance its operations. 
[[issuer]] Letter of Representations (ILOR) 
An agreement accepted by [[DTC]] from municipal and corporate issuers in which the [[issuer]] agrees to comply with the 
requirements stated in the Operational Arrangements, as they may be amended from time to time. [[DTC]] requires an [[issuer]] 
to submit an ILOR each time it submits a [[BEO]] or 144A issue for eligibility, if a BLOR is not already on file. 
Letter of Representations ([[LOR]]) 
Form agreements collected for book entry only issues which insure that the issue will comply with the procedures outlined 
in the [[OA]]. 
Offering  
An offering is when a corporation, municipality or government entity elects to bring a new issue to the market in order to 
raise capital. 
Offering Documents 
A formal written offer to sell [[securities]] that sets forth the plan for the proposed business enterprise (or the facts concerning 
an existing one) that an [[investor]] needs in order to make an informed investment decision. 
Office of Foreign [[Asset]] Control (OFAC) 
All issues are required to go through an OFAC check to determine if the [[issuer]] or country of origin is listed on OFAC. This 
check is done systemically twice during the [[Underwriting]] process. As soon as the [[issuer]] name is transmitted to [[DTCC]], a 
check is performed.  A second check is performed at Closing in case the status has changed from unmatched to potential 
match or a hit.  
[[participant]] [[underwriter]]  
A registered [[Member]] eligible for services at [[DTC]]. 
Paying Agent  
An entity, usually a bank or trust company, that is responsible for transmitting payments of interest and principal from an 
[[issuer]] to the security holder. 
Standard & Poor’s (S&P)  
A financial services company in which the stocks, corporate, and municipal [[Bonds]] are rated based on their risk profiles. 
[[Securities and Exchange Commission]] ([[SEC]])  
A government commission that regulates the [[securities]] markets and protects investors.  
[[syndicate]]  
A group of investment banks assembled by a lead manager to underwrite a new issue. 
[[transfer agent]]  
A financial institution that maintains records of investors, cancels and issues certificates, and deals with other related 
problems. 
[[underwriter]] 
An [[intermediary]] between an [[issuer]] of a security and the investing public. An [[underwriter]] can either be [[DTC]] [[participant]] or 
Correspondent who would clear the [[Underwriting]] deal using the Crediting [[participant]]. 
  

---

## Page 331

UW SOURCE: Eligibility Processing – Part 5 
Glossary 331 
[[Underwriting]]  
[[Underwriting]] is the process by which new issues are introduced to the [[securities]] industry. 
Uniform Security Identification Procedures ([[CUSIP]]) 
The identification number created by the American Banking Associations Committee on Uniform Security Identification 
Procedures ([[CUSIP]]) to uniquely identify issuers and issues of [[securities]] and financial instruments. The [[CUSIP]] number 
consists of nine digits; the first six digits identify the [[issuer]] and have been assigned to issuers in alphabetic sequence, and 
the next two characters (alphabetic or numeric) identify the issue. The ninth digit is the check digit.
